1→---
     2→name: lota-agent
     3→description: >
     4→  Start the autonomous LOTA agent in the background. The agent polls GitHub Issues
     5→  for assigned tasks, then spawns Claude Code to plan, execute, and complete them.
     6→  Use when the user says "lota-agent", "start agent", "autonomous mode", "launch agent",
     7→  or wants to run the autonomous agent.
     8→allowed-tools: Bash(node *), Bash(cd * && node *), Bash(kill *), Bash(sleep *), Bash(ps *), Bash(pkill *), Bash(git clone *), Bash(npm *), Bash(curl *), Bash(mkdir *)
     9→---
    10→
    11→# LOTA Agent Skill
    12→
    13→## What to do
    14→
    15→Run this EXACT sequence. Do NOT run any other commands.
    16→
    17→### Step 1: Ensure lota is built
    18→
    19→Check if the build exists. If not, clone and build automatically:
    20→
    21→```bash
    22→if [ ! -f ~/.lota/lota/dist/daemon.js ]; then
    23→  git clone https://github.com/xliry/lota.git ~/.lota/lota && cd ~/.lota/lota && npm install && npm run build
    24→fi
    25→```
    26→
    27→If npm is not found, install Node.js first:
    28→```bash
    29→curl -fsSL https://deb.nodesource.com/setup_20.x | bash - && apt-get install -y nodejs
    30→```
    31→
    32→### Step 2: Ensure .mcp.json exists
    33→
    34→If `.mcp.json` does not exist in the current project directory, ask the user for:
    35→1. GitHub Token (fine-grained PAT with Issues read/write)
    36→2. GitHub Repo (owner/repo format)
    37→3. Agent Name (default: dev-1)
    38→
    39→Then write `.mcp.json` with those values. If it already exists, skip this step.
    40→
    41→### Step 3: Kill any existing agent
    42→
    43→```bash
    44→pkill -f "node.*daemon" 2>/dev/null; true
    45→```
    46→
    47→Note: This may show "exit code 144" — that's normal (process was killed). Ignore it.
    48→
    49→### Step 4: Start agent in background
    50→
    51→Run with `run_in_background: true` and `timeout: 600000`:
    52→
    53→```bash
    54→cd ~/.lota/lota && node dist/daemon.js --interval 15 2>&1
    55→```
    56→
    57→### Step 5: Wait and read log file
    58→
    59→```bash
    60→sleep 5
    61→```
    62→
    63→Then use the **Read** tool to read `~/.lota/agent.log`.
    64→
    65→### Step 6: Report to user
    66→
    67→Show the log content and tell the user:
    68→- The agent name (from banner)
    69→- The poll interval
    70→- "Agent is running. Check logs anytime: `cat ~/.lota/agent.log`"
    71→
    72→That's ALL. Do NOT run diagnostics, version checks, or anything else.
    73→
```

1→---
     2→name: lota-hub
     3→description: >
     4→  LOTA admin dashboard. Create tasks, assign agents, send messages, check status,
     5→  and manage your agent workforce. Use when the user says "lota hub", "lota admin",
     6→  "send task", "check agents", "assign task", "create task", "manage agents",
     7→  or wants to manage agents and tasks.
     8→allowed-tools: mcp__lota__lota
     9→---
    10→
    11→# LOTA Hub — Admin Dashboard
    12→
    13→## What to do
    14→
    15→You are the LOTA admin interface. Help the user manage their agents interactively.
    16→
    17→### On launch, show the dashboard
    18→
    19→First, fetch current state:
    20→
    21→```
    22→lota("GET", "/sync")
    23→```
    24→
    25→Then display a summary:
    26→
    27→```
    28→LOTA Hub
    29→────────────────────────────
    30→  Tasks:    X pending, Y in-progress, Z completed
    31→  Messages: X unread
    32→────────────────────────────
    33→```
    34→
    35→### Available actions
    36→
    37→Ask the user what they want to do. Common actions:
    38→
    39→**Tasks**
    40→- Create task: `lota("POST", "/tasks", {"title": "...", "assign": "agent-name", "priority": "high|medium|low", "body": "..."})`
    41→- List tasks: `lota("GET", "/tasks")`
    42→- Check task: `lota("GET", "/tasks/<id>")`
    43→- Add comment: `lota("POST", "/tasks/<id>/comment", {"content": "..."})`
    44→
    45→**Messages**
    46→- Send DM: `lota("POST", "/messages", {"to": "agent-name", "content": "..."})`
    47→- Check messages: `lota("GET", "/messages")`
    48→- Reply: `lota("POST", "/messages/<id>/reply", {"content": "..."})`
    49→
    50→**Status**
    51→- Full sync: `lota("GET", "/sync")`
    52→- Filter tasks: `lota("GET", "/tasks?status=in-progress")`
    53→
    54→### Interaction style
    55→
    56→- Be conversational. Ask "What do you want to do?" after each action.
    57→- When creating tasks, ask for: title, agent to assign, priority, and description.
    58→- Show results in a clean, readable format.
    59→- Stay in hub mode — keep asking for next action until the user says they're done.
    60→
```

1→# lota
     2→
     3→Agent-to-agent communication over GitHub Issues. Zero infra — just a GitHub repo + PAT.
     4→
     5→## Get Started
     6→
     7→Paste this into your agent to set up LOTA automatically:
     8→
     9→```
    10→Set up LOTA — agent-to-agent communication over GitHub Issues. Zero infra, zero database.
    11→Tasks, messages, plans, and reports all live as GitHub Issues with labels.
    12→
    13→Step 1: Clone & build
    14→
    15→git clone https://github.com/xliry/lota.git ~/.lota/lota
    16→cd ~/.lota/lota && npm install && npm run build
    17→
    18→If ~/.lota/lota already exists: cd ~/.lota/lota && git pull && npm install && npm run build
    19→
    20→Requires Node.js 18+. If npm is not found, install Node.js first:
    21→curl -fsSL https://deb.nodesource.com/setup_20.x | bash - && apt-get install -y nodejs
    22→
    23→Step 2: Install skills
    24→
    25→Copy the LOTA skills into the current project so /lota-hub and /lota-agent work:
    26→
    27→mkdir -p .claude/skills/lota-agent .claude/skills/lota-hub
    28→cp ~/.lota/lota/.claude/skills/lota-agent/SKILL.md .claude/skills/lota-agent/SKILL.md
    29→cp ~/.lota/lota/.claude/skills/lota-hub/SKILL.md .claude/skills/lota-hub/SKILL.md
    30→cp ~/.lota/lota/.claude/settings.json .claude/settings.json
    31→
    32→Step 3: Configure
    33→
    34→Add to your project's .mcp.json (create if it doesn't exist):
    35→
    36→{
    37→  "mcpServers": {
    38→    "lota": {
    39→      "command": "node",
    40→      "args": ["~/.lota/lota/dist/index.js"],
    41→      "env": {
    42→        "GITHUB_TOKEN": "YOUR_GITHUB_PAT",
    43→        "GITHUB_REPO": "owner/repo-name",
    44→        "AGENT_NAME": "dev-1"
    45→      }
    46→    }
    47→  }
    48→}
    49→
    50→If .mcp.json already exists, merge the "lota" key into the existing mcpServers object.
    51→
    52→Ask the user for:
    53→1. GitHub Token — Fine-grained PAT with Issues read/write (Settings → Developer settings → Fine-grained tokens)
    54→2. GitHub Repo — A repo for agent communication (e.g. "username/my-agents"). If they don't have one, suggest creating a private repo.
    55→3. Agent Name — Their agent identity (default: "dev-1")
    56→
    57→Step 4: Restart & verify
    58→
    59→Tell the user to restart Claude Code so the MCP server loads and skills are recognized.
    60→After restart, test with:
    61→lota("GET", "/sync")
    62→
    63→Should return {"tasks": [], "messages": []} if the connection works.
    64→
    65→Step 5: Show what's possible
    66→
    67→Once connected, the user can:
    68→
    69→As Admin — create and assign tasks:
    70→  lota("POST", "/tasks", {"title": "Fix login bug", "assign": "dev-1", "priority": "high"})
    71→  lota("GET", "/tasks")
    72→  lota("POST", "/messages", {"to": "dev-1", "content": "Check the auth module"})
    73→
    74→As Agent — receive and complete tasks:
    75→  lota("GET", "/sync")
    76→  lota("POST", "/tasks/1/plan", {"goals": ["Investigate", "Fix", "Test"], "affected_files": ["auth.ts"], "effort": "medium"})
    77→  lota("POST", "/tasks/1/status", {"status": "in-progress"})
    78→  lota("POST", "/tasks/1/complete", {"summary": "Fixed the bug by..."})
    79→
    80→Commands after setup:
    81→- /lota-hub — admin dashboard: create tasks, assign agents, send messages
    82→- /lota-agent — start autonomous agent daemon that polls and executes tasks
    83→
    84→How it works: GitHub Issues = task database. Labels = state machine.
    85→- "task" label = it's a task
    86→- "agent:dev-1" = assigned to dev-1
    87→- "status:assigned" → "status:in-progress" → "status:completed"
    88→- "dm" label = direct message between agents
    89→- Comments = plans, reports, structured metadata
    90→
    91→No server. No database. Just GitHub Issues + labels.
    92→```
    93→
    94→After setup, use `/lota-hub` to manage agents or `/lota-agent` to start autonomous mode.
    95→
    96→---
    97→
    98→## For Admins
    99→
   100→Create tasks, assign to agents, send messages:
   101→
   102→```
   103→lota("POST", "/tasks", {"title": "Implement auth", "assign": "dev-1", "priority": "high"})
   104→lota("GET", "/tasks")
   105→lota("GET", "/tasks/1")
   106→lota("POST", "/messages", {"to": "dev-1", "content": "Start on auth ASAP"})
   107→```
   108→
   109→## For Agents
   110→
   111→Check for work, plan, execute, report:
   112→
   113→```
   114→lota("GET", "/sync")
   115→lota("POST", "/tasks/1/plan", {"goals": ["Setup JWT", "Add middleware"], "affected_files": ["auth.ts"], "effort": "medium"})
   116→lota("POST", "/tasks/1/status", {"status": "in-progress"})
   117→# ... do the work ...
   118→lota("POST", "/tasks/1/complete", {"summary": "Implemented JWT auth with refresh tokens"})
   119→```
   120→
   121→## Autonomous Mode
   122→
   123→Run `/lota-agent` in Claude Code to start the autonomous daemon. Or run directly:
   124→
   125→```bash
   126→node ~/.lota/lota/dist/daemon.js --interval 15
   127→```
   128→
   129→Options:
   130→- `-i, --interval <sec>` — Poll interval in seconds (default: 15)
   131→- `-1, --once` — Run once then exit
   132→- `-m, --model <model>` — Claude model (default: sonnet)
   133→- `-c, --config <path>` — MCP config file (default: .mcp.json)
   134→
   135→## How It Works
   136→
   137→```
   138→┌─────────┐     GitHub Issues      ┌─────────┐
   139→│  Admin   │ ──── tasks/messages ──→│  Agent   │
   140→│ (human)  │ ←── reports/replies ───│ (Claude) │
   141→└─────────┘     labels = state      └─────────┘
   142→```
   143→
   144→- **Tasks** = GitHub Issues with `task` label
   145→- **Status** = Labels (`status:assigned`, `status:in-progress`, `status:completed`)
   146→- **Assignment** = Labels (`agent:dev-1`)
   147→- **Messages** = Issues with `dm` label
   148→- **Plans & Reports** = Issue comments with structured metadata
   149→
   150→No database. No server. Just GitHub.
   151→
   152→## API Reference
   153→
   154→| Method | Path | Description |
   155→|--------|------|-------------|
   156→| GET | `/sync` | All pending work (tasks + messages) |
   157→| GET | `/tasks` | My assigned tasks |
   158→| GET | `/tasks?status=X` | Filter by status |
   159→| GET | `/tasks/:id` | Task detail + comments |
   160→| POST | `/tasks` | Create task `{title, assign?, priority?, body?}` |
   161→| POST | `/tasks/:id/plan` | Save plan `{goals[], affected_files[], effort}` |
   162→| POST | `/tasks/:id/status` | Update status `{status}` |
   163→| POST | `/tasks/:id/complete` | Complete `{summary, modified_files?, new_files?}` |
   164→| POST | `/tasks/:id/comment` | Add comment `{content}` |
   165→| GET | `/messages` | My unread DMs |
   166→| POST | `/messages` | Send DM `{to, content}` |
   167→| POST | `/messages/:id/reply` | Reply to DM `{content}` |
   168→
   169→## Config
   170→
   171→3 env vars. That's it.
   172→
   173→| Var | Description |
   174→|-----|-------------|
   175→| `GITHUB_TOKEN` | Fine-grained PAT with Issues read/write |
   176→| `GITHUB_REPO` | `owner/repo` format |
   177→| `AGENT_NAME` | Your agent identity (e.g. `dev-1`) |
   178→
   179→
```

1→# LOTA — Agent Communication over GitHub Issues
     2→
     3→You are connected to the LOTA platform via the `lota` MCP tool.
     4→LOTA enables agent-to-agent communication using GitHub Issues as the backend — zero infra required.
     5→
     6→## Quick Start
     7→
     8→Use the `lota()` MCP tool. It takes 3 parameters:
     9→- `method`: GET, POST
    10→- `path`: API endpoint
    11→- `body`: Request body (optional, for POST)
    12→
    13→## Config (3 env vars)
    14→
    15→- `GITHUB_TOKEN` — GitHub PAT with Issues read/write
    16→- `GITHUB_REPO` — "owner/repo" format
    17→- `AGENT_NAME` — your agent identity (e.g. "dev-1")
    18→
    19→## Agent Workflow
    20→
    21→1. **Check work**: `lota("GET", "/sync")` — see pending tasks & messages
    22→2. **Plan**: `lota("POST", "/tasks/{id}/plan", {goals, affected_files, effort})`
    23→3. **Start**: `lota("POST", "/tasks/{id}/status", {status: "in-progress"})`
    24→4. **Do the work**: Write code, run tests, iterate
    25→5. **Complete**: `lota("POST", "/tasks/{id}/complete", {summary: "..."})`
    26→
```

1→#!/usr/bin/env node
     2→import { McpServer } from "@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/mcp.js";
     3→import { StdioServerTransport } from "@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/stdio.js";
     4→import { z } from "zod";
     5→import { lota, AGENT_NAME, GITHUB_REPO } from "./github.js";
     6→
     7→const server = new McpServer({ name: "lota", version: "3.0.0" });
     8→
     9→// ── Single tool: lota() ─────────────────────────────────────────
    10→
    11→const API_DOCS = `LOTA — agent-to-agent communication over GitHub Issues.
    12→Your agent: "${AGENT_NAME}"  Repo: "${GITHUB_REPO}"
    13→
    14→ENDPOINTS:
    15→  GET  /tasks                    → my assigned tasks
    16→  GET  /tasks?status=X           → filter by status
    17→  GET  /tasks/:id                → task detail + comments
    18→  POST /tasks                    → create {title, assign?, priority?, body?, workspace?}
    19→  POST /tasks/:id/plan           → save plan {goals[], affected_files[], effort}
    20→  POST /tasks/:id/status         → update {status: assigned|in-progress|completed}
    21→  POST /tasks/:id/complete       → report {summary, modified_files?, new_files?}
    22→  POST /tasks/:id/comment        → add comment {content}
    23→  GET  /sync                     → all pending work (tasks)`;
    24→
    25→server.tool(
    26→  "lota",
    27→  API_DOCS,
    28→  {
    29→    method: z.enum(["GET", "POST", "PATCH", "PUT", "DELETE"]).describe("HTTP method"),
    30→    path: z.string().describe("API path (e.g. /api/tasks)"),
    31→    body: z.record(z.unknown()).optional().describe("Request body (for POST/PATCH/PUT)"),
    32→  },
    33→  async ({ method, path, body }) => {
    34→    try {
    35→      const result = await lota(method, path, body);
    36→      return {
    37→        content: [{
    38→          type: "text" as const,
    39→          text: typeof result === "string" ? result : JSON.stringify(result, null, 2),
    40→        }],
    41→      };
    42→    } catch (e) {
    43→      return {
    44→        content: [{ type: "text" as const, text: `Error: ${(e as Error).message}` }],
    45→        isError: true,
    46→      };
    47→    }
    48→  }
    49→);
    50→
    51→// ── Connect ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    52→
    53→const transport = new StdioServerTransport();
    54→await server.connect(transport);
    55→
```

1→#!/usr/bin/env node
     2→import { spawn, type ChildProcess } from "node:child_process";
     3→import { existsSync, readFileSync, mkdirSync, appendFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
     4→import { resolve, join } from "node:path";
     5→import { lota } from "./github.js";
     6→
     7→
     8→// ── Config ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
     9→
    10→interface AgentConfig {
    11→  configPath: string;
    12→  model: string;
    13→  interval: number;
    14→  once: boolean;
    15→  agentName: string;
    16→  githubToken: string;
    17→  githubRepo: string;
    18→}
    19→
    20→function parseArgs(): AgentConfig {
    21→  const args = process.argv.slice(2);
    22→  let interval = 15;
    23→  let once = false;
    24→  let mcpConfig = "";
    25→  let model = "sonnet";
    26→
    27→  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i++) {
    28→    switch (args[i]) {
    29→      case "--interval": case "-i": interval = parseInt(args[++i], 10); break;
    30→      case "--once": case "-1": once = true; break;
    31→      case "--config": case "-c": mcpConfig = args[++i]; break;
    32→      case "--model": case "-m": model = args[++i]; break;
    33→      case "--help": case "-h":
    34→        console.log(`Usage: lota-agent [options]
    35→
    36→Autonomous LOTA agent (GitHub-backed).
    37→Listens for assigned tasks, plans, executes, and reports.
    38→
    39→Options:
    40→  -c, --config <path>   MCP config file (default: .mcp.json)
    41→  -m, --model <model>   Claude model (default: sonnet)
    42→  -i, --interval <sec>  Poll interval in seconds (default: 15)
    43→  -1, --once            Run once then exit
    44→  -h, --help            Show this help`);
    45→        process.exit(0);
    46→    }
    47→  }
    48→
    49→  // Find .mcp.json — search upward from cwd, then $HOME
    50→  function findMcpConfig(): string {
    51→    let dir = process.cwd();
    52→    while (true) {
    53→      const candidate = join(dir, ".mcp.json");
    54→      if (existsSync(candidate)) return candidate;
    55→      const parent = resolve(dir, "..");
    56→      if (parent === dir) break;
    57→      dir = parent;
    58→    }
    59→    const home = resolve(process.env.HOME || "~", ".mcp.json");
    60→    if (existsSync(home)) return home;
    61→    return "";
    62→  }
    63→  const configPath = mcpConfig ? resolve(mcpConfig) : findMcpConfig();
    64→
    65→  if (!configPath) {
    66→    console.error("Error: .mcp.json not found. Run: lota-agent --help");
    67→    process.exit(1);
    68→  }
    69→
    70→  // Read credentials from .mcp.json
    71→  let githubToken = "", githubRepo = "", agentName = "";
    72→  try {
    73→    const cfg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(configPath, "utf-8"));
    74→    const env = cfg.mcpServers?.lota?.env || {};
    75→    [REDACTED] || "";
    76→    githubRepo = env.GITHUB_REPO || "";
    77→    agentName = env.AGENT_NAME || "";
    78→  } catch (e) {
    79→    console.error(`Error reading ${configPath}: ${(e as Error).message}`);
    80→    process.exit(1);
    81→  }
    82→
    83→  if (!githubToken) { console.error("Error: GITHUB_TOKEN missing in .mcp.json"); process.exit(1); }
    84→  if (!githubRepo) { console.error("Error: GITHUB_REPO missing in .mcp.json"); process.exit(1); }
    85→  if (!agentName) { console.error("Error: AGENT_NAME missing in .mcp.json"); process.exit(1); }
    86→
    87→  return { configPath, model, interval, once, agentName, githubToken, githubRepo };
    88→}
    89→
    90→// ── Logging (stdout + file) ──────────────────────────────────────
    91→
    92→const LOG_DIR = join(process.env.HOME || "~", ".lota", "lota");
    93→const LOG_FILE = join(LOG_DIR, "agent.log");
    94→mkdirSync(LOG_DIR, { recursive: true });
    95→writeFileSync(LOG_FILE, ""); // clear on start
    96→
    97→const time = () => new Date().toLocaleTimeString("en-US", { hour12: false });
    98→
    99→function out(msg: string, plain: string) {
   100→  console.log(msg);
   101→  appendFileSync(LOG_FILE, `${plain}\n`);
   102→}
   103→
   104→const PRE = "\x1b[36m[lota-agent]\x1b[0m";
   105→const log = (msg: string) => out(`${PRE} \x1b[90m${time()}\x1b[0m ${msg}`, `[${time()}] ${msg}`);
   106→const ok = (msg: string) => out(`${PRE} \x1b[90m${time()}\x1b[0m \x1b[32m✓ ${msg}\x1b[0m`, `[${time()}] ✓ ${msg}`);
   107→const dim = (msg: string) => out(`${PRE} \x1b[90m${time()} ${msg}\x1b[0m`, `[${time()}] ${msg}`);
   108→const err = (msg: string) => out(`${PRE} \x1b[90m${time()}\x1b[0m \x1b[31m✗ ${msg}\x1b[0m`, `[${time()}] ✗ ${msg}`);
   109→
   110→// ── Pre-check (zero-cost, no LLM) ──────────────────────────────
   111→
   112→interface WorkData {
   113→  tasks: { id: number; title: string; status: string; body?: string; workspace?: string }[];
   114→}
   115→
   116→async function checkForWork(config: AgentConfig): Promise<WorkData> {
   117→  // Set env vars so github.ts can use them
   118→  process.env.[REDACTED];
   119→  process.env.GITHUB_REPO = config.githubRepo;
   120→  process.env.AGENT_NAME = config.agentName;
   121→
   122→  const data = await lota("GET", "/sync") as { tasks: WorkData["tasks"] };
   123→  return { tasks: data.tasks || [] };
   124→}
   125→
   126→// ── Prompt ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   127→
   128→function buildPrompt(agentName: string, work: WorkData, config: AgentConfig): string {
   129→  const repoOwner = config.githubRepo.split("/")[0] || agentName;
   130→  const lines = [
   131→    `You are autonomous LOTA agent "${agentName}". Use the lota() MCP tool for all API calls.`,
   132→    "",
   133→    "── RULES ──",
   134→    "  GIT COMMIT RULES (MUST follow):",
   135→    `    - git config user.name "${agentName}"`,
   136→    `    - git config user.email "${repoOwner}@users.noreply.github.com"`,
   137→    "    - NEVER put tokens or credentials in git remote URLs",
   138→    "    - Use `git push` directly — GITHUB_TOKEN is already in your environment",
   139→    "    - If remote URL needs auth, use: git remote set-url origin https://x-access-token:${GITHUB_TOKEN}@github.com/OWNER/REPO.git",
   140→    "",
   141→    "  WORKSPACE RULES:",
   142→    "    - If a task has a workspace path, that is your working directory. The project is ALREADY there.",
   143→    "    - NEVER git clone a repo that already exists locally.",
   144→    "    - NEVER work in /tmp/ if a workspace path is provided.",
   145→    "    - Use Write/Edit tools for file operations, NOT cat/heredoc via Bash.",
   146→  ];
   147→
   148→  if (work.tasks.length) {
   149→    lines.push("", "── ASSIGNED TASKS ──");
   150→    for (const t of work.tasks) {
   151→      lines.push(`  Task #${t.id}: ${t.title || "(untitled)"}`);
   152→      if (t.workspace) {
   153→        lines.push(`  Workspace: ${t.workspace} (project is here — DO NOT clone)`);
   154→      }
   155→      if (t.body) {
   156→        lines.push("", "  ── TASK BODY ──", t.body, "  ── END BODY ──");
   157→      }
   158→    }
   159→    lines.push(
   160→      "",
   161→      "  IMPORTANT: Before starting any task, ALWAYS read the full task details and comments first:",
   162→      `    lota("GET", "/tasks/<id>")`,
   163→      "  Comments may contain critical updates, requirement changes, or tech stack decisions.",
   164→      "",
   165→      "  For each task:",
   166→      `    1. Read full details: lota("GET", "/tasks/<id>") — check body AND comments for updates`,
   167→      `    2. Save plan: lota("POST", "/tasks/<id>/plan", {"goals": [...], "affected_files": [], "effort": "medium"})`,
   168→      `    3. Set status: lota("POST", "/tasks/<id>/status", {"status": "in-progress"})`,
   169→      "    4. Execute: read files, write code, run tests.",
   170→      `    5. Complete: lota("POST", "/tasks/<id>/complete", {"summary": "...", "modified_files": [], "new_files": []})`,
   171→    );
   172→  }
   173→
   174→  return lines.join("\n");
   175→}
   176→
   177→// ── Event formatter (stream-json → readable log) ────────────────
   178→
   179→function formatEvent(event: any) {
   180→  const t = time();
   181→  const write = (icon: string, msg: string) => {
   182→    const plain = `[${t}] ${icon} ${msg}`;
   183→    const colored = `${PRE} \x1b[90m${t}\x1b[0m ${icon} ${msg}`;
   184→    console.log(colored);
   185→    appendFileSync(LOG_FILE, `${plain}\n`);
   186→  };
   187→
   188→  // Tool use (agent calling a tool)
   189→  if (event.type === "content_block_start" && event.content_block?.type === "tool_use") {
   190→    const name = event.content_block.name || "unknown";
   191→    write("🔧", `Tool: ${name}`);
   192→    return;
   193→  }
   194→
   195→  // Tool result
   196→  if (event.type === "result" && event.subtype === "tool_result") {
   197→    return; // skip verbose tool results
   198→  }
   199→
   200→  // Assistant text
   201→  if (event.type === "assistant" && event.message?.content) {
   202→    for (const block of event.message.content) {
   203→      if (block.type === "tool_use") {
   204→        const name = block.name || "";
   205→        const input = block.input || {};
   206→
   207→        if (name === "Write" || name === "Edit") {
   208→          write("📝", `${name}: ${input.file_path || ""}`);
   209→        } else if (name === "Read") {
   210→          write("📖", `Read: ${input.file_path || ""}`);
   211→        } else if (name === "Bash") {
   212→          const cmd = (input.command || "").slice(0, 120);
   213→          write("💻", `Bash: ${cmd}`);
   214→        } else if (name === "Glob" || name === "Grep") {
   215→          write("🔍", `${name}: ${input.pattern || ""}`);
   216→        } else if (name.startsWith("mcp__lota")) {
   217→          const method = input.method || "";
   218→          const path = input.path || "";
   219→          write("🔗", `LOTA: ${method} ${path}`);
   220→        } else {
   221→          write("🔧", `${name}`);
   222→        }
   223→      } else if (block.type === "text") {
   224→        const text = (block.text || "").slice(0, 200);
   225→        if (text.trim()) {
   226→          write("💬", text.replace(/\n/g, " ").trim());
   227→        }
   228→      }
   229→    }
   230→    return;
   231→  }
   232→
   233→  // System/result messages
   234→  if (event.type === "result") {
   235→    const cost = event.cost_usd ? `$${event.cost_usd.toFixed(4)}` : "";
   236→    const dur = event.duration_ms ? `${(event.duration_ms / 1000).toFixed(1)}s` : "";
   237→    const turns = event.num_turns || 0;
   238→    write("✅", `Done — ${turns} turns, ${dur}, ${cost}`);
   239→    return;
   240→  }
   241→}
   242→
   243→// ── Claude subprocess ───────────────────────────────────────────
   244→
   245→let currentProcess: ChildProcess | null = null;
   246→let busy = false;
   247→
   248→function runClaude(config: AgentConfig, work: WorkData): Promise<number> {
   249→  if (busy) {
   250→    dim("Already running, skipping...");
   251→    return Promise.resolve(0);
   252→  }
   253→  busy = true;
   254→
   255→  return new Promise((resolve) => {
   256→    // Clean env to prevent nested Claude session errors
   257→    const cleanEnv = { ...process.env };
   258→    for (const key of Object.keys(cleanEnv)) {
   259→      if (key.startsWith("CLAUDE_CODE") || key === "CLAUDECODE" || key === "CLAUDE_SHELL_SESSION_ID") {
   260→        delete cleanEnv[key];
   261→      }
   262→    }
   263→
   264→    // Set GitHub env vars for the Claude subprocess
   265→    cleanEnv.[REDACTED];
   266→    cleanEnv.GITHUB_REPO = config.githubRepo;
   267→    cleanEnv.AGENT_NAME = config.agentName;
   268→
   269→    const isRoot = process.getuid?.() === 0;
   270→    const args = [
   271→      "--print",
   272→      "--verbose",
   273→      "--output-format", "stream-json",
   274→      ...(isRoot ? [] : ["--dangerously-skip-permissions"]),
   275→      "--model", config.model,
   276→      "--mcp-config", config.configPath,
   277→      "-p", buildPrompt(config.agentName, work, config),
   278→    ];
   279→
   280→    if (isRoot) {
   281→      dim("Running as root — skipping --dangerously-skip-permissions");
   282→    }
   283→
   284→    // Use workspace from first task as cwd if available
   285→    const taskWorkspace = work.tasks[0]?.workspace;
   286→    const workingDir = taskWorkspace && existsSync(taskWorkspace) ? taskWorkspace : process.cwd();
   287→    if (taskWorkspace) {
   288→      if (existsSync(taskWorkspace)) {
   289→        ok(`Workspace: ${taskWorkspace}`);
   290→      } else {
   291→        err(`Workspace not found: ${taskWorkspace} — using cwd`);
   292→      }
   293→    }
   294→
   295→    const child = spawn("claude", args, {
   296→      stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"],
   297→      cwd: workingDir,
   298→      env: cleanEnv,
   299→    });
   300→
   301→    currentProcess = child;
   302→
   303→    let jsonBuffer = "";
   304→
   305→    child.stdout?.on("data", (d: Buffer) => {
   306→      jsonBuffer += d.toString();
   307→      const lines = jsonBuffer.split("\n");
   308→      jsonBuffer = lines.pop() || "";
   309→
   310→      for (const line of lines) {
   311→        if (!line.trim()) continue;
   312→        try {
   313→          const event = JSON.parse(line);
   314→          formatEvent(event);
   315→        } catch {
   316→          // Not JSON, log raw
   317→          console.log(`  ${line}`);
   318→          appendFileSync(LOG_FILE, `  ${line}\n`);
   319→        }
   320→      }
   321→    });
   322→
   323→    child.stderr?.on("data", (d: Buffer) => {
   324→      const text = d.toString();
   325→      for (const line of text.split("\n")) {
   326→        if (line.trim()) {
   327→          appendFileSync(LOG_FILE, `  [stderr] ${line}\n`);
   328→        }
   329→      }
   330→    });
   331→
   332→    child.on("close", (code) => {
   333→      currentProcess = null;
   334→      busy = false;
   335→      resolve(code ?? 1);
   336→    });
   337→
   338→    child.on("error", (e) => {
   339→      currentProcess = null;
   340→      busy = false;
   341→      if ((e as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") {
   342→        err("'claude' not found. Install: npm i -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code");
   343→      } else {
   344→        err(`Spawn error: ${e.message}`);
   345→      }
   346→      resolve(1);
   347→    });
   348→  });
   349→}
   350→
   351→// ── Shutdown ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   352→
   353→let stopped = false;
   354→let sleepResolve: (() => void) | null = null;
   355→
   356→for (const sig of ["SIGINT", "SIGTERM"] as const) {
   357→  process.on(sig, () => {
   358→    if (stopped) {
   359→      if (currentProcess) currentProcess.kill("SIGKILL");
   360→      process.exit(0);
   361→    }
   362→    stopped = true;
   363→    log("Shutting down...");
   364→    if (currentProcess) {
   365→      currentProcess.kill("SIGTERM");
   366→      setTimeout(() => {
   367→        if (currentProcess) currentProcess.kill("SIGKILL");
   368→        process.exit(0);
   369→      }, 5000);
   370→    }
   371→    sleepResolve?.();
   372→  });
   373→}
   374→
   375→const MS_PER_SECOND = 1000;
   376→
   377→function sleep(sec: number): Promise<void> {
   378→  return new Promise((r) => {
   379→    sleepResolve = r;
   380→    setTimeout(() => { sleepResolve = null; r(); }, sec * MS_PER_SECOND);
   381→  });
   382→}
   383→
   384→// ── Main ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   385→
   386→async function main() {
   387→  const config = parseArgs();
   388→
   389→  const banner = [
   390→    "",
   391→    "  ┌─────────────────────────┐",
   392→    "  │      LOTA Agent         │",
   393→    "  └─────────────────────────┘",
   394→    `  agent:    ${config.agentName}`,
   395→    `  model:    ${config.model}`,
   396→    `  config:   ${config.configPath}`,
   397→    `  interval: ${config.interval}s`,
   398→    `  log:      ${LOG_FILE}`,
   399→    "",
   400→  ];
   401→  for (const line of banner) {
   402→    console.log(line);
   403→    appendFileSync(LOG_FILE, `${line}\n`);
   404→  }
   405→
   406→  log("━━━ Agent active, waiting for tasks ━━━");
   407→  console.log("");
   408→
   409→  // Main loop: poll → check → spawn → sleep
   410→  while (!stopped) {
   411→    log("Checking for work...");
   412→
   413→    let work: WorkData;
   414→    try {
   415→      work = await checkForWork(config);
   416→    } catch (e) {
   417→      err(`Pre-check failed: ${(e as Error).message}`);
   418→      if (config.once) break;
   419→      await sleep(config.interval);
   420→      continue;
   421→    }
   422→
   423→    const taskCount = work.tasks.length;
   424→
   425→    if (taskCount === 0) {
   426→      dim(`No pending work — skipped Claude spawn`);
   427→    } else {
   428→      ok(`Found ${taskCount} task(s)`);
   429→      for (const t of work.tasks) {
   430→        dim(`  → #${t.id}: ${t.title}`);
   431→      }
   432→      console.log("  ─────────────────────────────────────");
   433→
   434→      const cycleStart = Date.now();
   435→      const code = await runClaude(config, work);
   436→      const elapsed = Math.round((Date.now() - cycleStart) / 1000);
   437→
   438→      console.log("  ─────────────────────────────────────");
   439→      if (code === 0) {
   440→        ok(`Cycle complete in ${elapsed}s (${taskCount} task(s))`);
   441→      } else {
   442→        err(`Claude exited with code ${code} after ${elapsed}s`);
   443→      }
   444→    }
   445→
   446→    if (config.once) break;
   447→
   448→    dim(`Polling in ${config.interval}s...`);
   449→    await sleep(config.interval);
   450→  }
   451→}
   452→
   453→main();
   454→
```

1→export const [REDACTED] || "";
     2→export const GITHUB_REPO = process.env.GITHUB_REPO || "";
     3→export const AGENT_NAME = process.env.AGENT_NAME || "";
     4→
     5→const token = () => process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN || "";
     6→const repo = () => process.env.GITHUB_REPO || "";
     7→const agent = () => process.env.AGENT_NAME || "";
     8→
     9→// ── Constants ────────────────────────────────────────────────
    10→
    11→const LABEL = {
    12→  TYPE: "task",
    13→  AGENT: "agent:",
    14→  STATUS: "status:",
    15→  PRIORITY: "priority:",
    16→} as const;
    17→
    18→const META_VERSION = "v1";
    19→
    20→export type TaskStatus = "assigned" | "in-progress" | "completed";
    21→export type TaskPriority = "low" | "medium" | "high";
    22→
    23→export interface Task {
    24→  id: number;
    25→  number: number;
    26→  title: string;
    27→  status: string;
    28→  assignee: string | null;
    29→  priority: string | null;
    30→  labels: string[];
    31→  body: string;
    32→  workspace: string | null;
    33→}
    34→
    35→export interface LotaError {
    36→  error: string;
    37→  code: string;
    38→  details?: unknown;
    39→}
    40→
    41→// ── GitHub API fetch wrapper (with retry + backoff) ─────────
    42→
    43→const MAX_RETRIES = 3;
    44→const BASE_DELAY_MS = 1000;
    45→
    46→async function gh(path: string, opts: RequestInit = {}): Promise<unknown> {
    47→  let lastError: Error | null = null;
    48→
    49→  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < MAX_RETRIES; attempt++) {
    50→    try {
    51→      const res = await fetch(`https://api.github.com${path}`, {
    52→        ...opts,
    53→        headers: {
    54→          Accept: "application/vnd.github+json",
    55→          Authorization: `Bearer ${token()}`,
    56→          "X-GitHub-Api-Version": "2022-11-28",
    57→          "Content-Type": "application/json",
    58→          ...(opts.headers as Record<string, string> || {}),
    59→        },
    60→      });
    61→
    62→      // Rate limit — retry with backoff
    63→      if (res.status === 403 || res.status === 429) {
    64→        const retryAfter = res.headers.get("retry-after");
    65→        const delay = retryAfter ? parseInt(retryAfter, 10) * 1000 : BASE_DELAY_MS * Math.pow(2, attempt);
    66→        await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, delay));
    67→        continue;
    68→      }
    69→
    70→      const text = await res.text();
    71→      if (!res.ok) {
    72→        throw Object.assign(
    73→          new Error(`GitHub ${opts.method || "GET"} ${path} -> ${res.status}: ${text}`),
    74→          { status: res.status }
    75→        );
    76→      }
    77→
    78→      try { return JSON.parse(text); } catch { return text; }
    79→    } catch (e) {
    80→      lastError = e as Error;
    81→      // Only retry on network errors, not on 4xx
    82→      if ((e as { status?: number }).status && (e as { status?: number }).status! < 500) throw e;
    83→      if (attempt < MAX_RETRIES - 1) {
    84→        await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, BASE_DELAY_MS * Math.pow(2, attempt)));
    85→      }
    86→    }
    87→  }
    88→
    89→  throw lastError || new Error(`GitHub request failed after ${MAX_RETRIES} retries`);
    90→}
    91→
    92→// ── Metadata helpers (versioned) ────────────────────────────
    93→
    94→function parseMetadata(body: string, type: string): Record<string, unknown> | null {
    95→  // Try versioned format first: <!-- lota:v1:plan {...} -->
    96→  const vRe = new RegExp(`<!-- lota:${META_VERSION}:${type} (\\{.*?\\}) -->`, "s");
    97→  const vMatch = body.match(vRe);
    98→  if (vMatch) {
    99→    try { return JSON.parse(vMatch[1]); } catch { /* fall through */ }
   100→  }
   101→  // Fallback: legacy format <!-- lota:plan {...} -->
   102→  const re = new RegExp(`<!-- lota:${type} (\\{.*?\\}) -->`, "s");
   103→  const m = body.match(re);
   104→  if (!m) return null;
   105→  try { return JSON.parse(m[1]); } catch { return null; }
   106→}
   107→
   108→function formatMetadata(type: string, data: Record<string, unknown>, humanText: string): string {
   109→  return `${humanText}\n\n<!-- lota:${META_VERSION}:${type} ${JSON.stringify(data)} -->`;
   110→}
   111→
   112→function parseBodyMeta(body: string): Record<string, unknown> {
   113→  // Try versioned first
   114→  const vMatch = body.match(new RegExp(`<!-- lota:${META_VERSION}:meta (\\{.*?\\}) -->`, "s"));
   115→  if (vMatch) { try { return JSON.parse(vMatch[1]); } catch { /* fall through */ } }
   116→  // Fallback legacy
   117→  const m = body.match(/<!-- lota:meta (\{.*?\}) -->/s);
   118→  if (!m) return {};
   119→  try { return JSON.parse(m[1]); } catch { return {}; }
   120→}
   121→
   122→// ── Label helpers ───────────────────────────────────────────
   123→
   124→async function swapLabels(issueNumber: number, prefix: string, newLabel: string): Promise<void> {
   125→  // Use single PATCH to replace all labels atomically (instead of DELETE + POST)
   126→  const issue = await gh(`/repos/${repo()}/issues/${issueNumber}`) as { labels: { name: string }[] };
   127→  const kept = issue.labels.filter(l => !l.name.startsWith(prefix)).map(l => l.name);
   128→  kept.push(newLabel);
   129→  await gh(`/repos/${repo()}/issues/${issueNumber}/labels`, {
   130→    method: "PUT",
   131→    body: JSON.stringify({ labels: kept }),
   132→  });
   133→}
   134→
   135→type GhIssue = { number: number; title: string; body?: string; labels: { name: string }[] };
   136→
   137→function extractFromIssue(issue: GhIssue): Task {
   138→  const labels = issue.labels.map(l => l.name);
   139→  const status = labels.find(l => l.startsWith(LABEL.STATUS))?.slice(LABEL.STATUS.length) || "unknown";
   140→  const assignee = labels.find(l => l.startsWith(LABEL.AGENT))?.slice(LABEL.AGENT.length) || null;
   141→  const priority = labels.find(l => l.startsWith(LABEL.PRIORITY))?.slice(LABEL.PRIORITY.length) || null;
   142→  const meta = parseBodyMeta(issue.body || "");
   143→  const workspace = (meta.workspace as string) || null;
   144→  return { id: issue.number, number: issue.number, title: issue.title, status, assignee, priority, labels, body: issue.body || "", workspace };
   145→}
   146→
   147→// ── Router ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
   148→
   149→type Handler = (params: Record<string, string>, query: URLSearchParams, body?: Record<string, unknown>) => Promise<unknown>;
   150→
   151→interface Route {
   152→  method: string;
   153→  pattern: RegExp;
   154→  paramNames: string[];
   155→  handler: Handler;
   156→}
   157→
   158→function route(method: string, path: string, handler: Handler): Route {
   159→  const paramNames: string[] = [];
   160→  const regexStr = path.replace(/:(\w+)/g, (_, name) => {
   161→    paramNames.push(name);
   162→    return "(\\w+)";
   163→  });
   164→  return { method, pattern: new RegExp(`^${regexStr}$`), paramNames, handler };
   165→}
   166→
   167→// ── Handlers ────────────────────────────────────────────────
   168→
   169→const getTasks: Handler = async (_params, query) => {
   170→  const status = query.get("status");
   171→  const labels = status
   172→    ? `${LABEL.TYPE},${LABEL.STATUS}${status}`
   173→    : `${LABEL.TYPE},${LABEL.AGENT}${agent()}`;
   174→  const issues = await gh(`/repos/${repo()}/issues?labels=${encodeURIComponent(labels)}&state=open`) as GhIssue[];
   175→  return issues.map(extractFromIssue);
   176→};
   177→
   178→const getTask: Handler = async (params) => {
   179→  const { id } = params;
   180→  const [issue, comments] = await Promise.all([
   181→    gh(`/repos/${repo()}/issues/${id}`) as Promise<GhIssue>,
   182→    gh(`/repos/${repo()}/issues/${id}/comments`) as Promise<Array<{ body: string; created_at: string; user: { login: string } }>>,
   183→  ]);
   184→  const task = extractFromIssue(issue);
   185→  const plan = comments.map(c => parseMetadata(c.body, "plan")).find(Boolean) || null;
   186→  const report = comments.map(c => parseMetadata(c.body, "report")).find(Boolean) || null;
   187→  return { ...task, plan, report, comments: comments.map(c => ({ body: c.body, created_at: c.created_at, user: c.user.login })) };
   188→};
   189→
   190→const createTask: Handler = async (_params, _query, body) => {
   191→  const { title, assign, priority, body: taskBody, workspace } = body as {
   192→    title: string; assign?: string; priority?: string; body?: string; workspace?: string;
   193→  };
   194→  const labels = [LABEL.TYPE, `${LABEL.AGENT}${assign || agent()}`, `${LABEL.STATUS}assigned`];
   195→  if (priority) labels.push(`${LABEL.PRIORITY}${priority}`);
   196→  let finalBody = taskBody || "";
   197→  if (workspace) {
   198→    finalBody += `\n\n<!-- lota:${META_VERSION}:meta ${JSON.stringify({ workspace })} -->`;
   199→  }
   200→  return await gh(`/repos/${repo()}/issues`, {
   201→    method: "POST",
   202→    body: JSON.stringify({ title, body: finalBody, labels }),
   203→  });
   204→};
   205→
   206→const savePlan: Handler = async (params, _query, body) => {
   207→  const { id } = params;
   208→  const { goals, affected_files, effort, notes } = body as {
   209→    goals: string[]; affected_files?: string[]; effort?: string; notes?: string;
   210→  };
   211→  const humanText = `## Plan\n${goals.map(g => `- ${g}`).join("\n")}${effort ? `\nEstimated effort: ${effort}` : ""}${notes ? `\n\n${notes}` : ""}`;
   212→  const comment = formatMetadata("plan", { goals, affected_files: affected_files || [], effort: effort || "medium", notes }, humanText);
   213→  return await gh(`/repos/${repo()}/issues/${id}/comments`, {
   214→    method: "POST",
   215→    body: JSON.stringify({ body: comment }),
   216→  });
   217→};
   218→
   219→const updateStatus: Handler = async (params, _query, body) => {
   220→  const id = Number(params.id);
   221→  const { status } = body as { status: string };
   222→  await swapLabels(id, LABEL.STATUS, `${LABEL.STATUS}${status}`);
   223→  if (status === "completed") {
   224→    await gh(`/repos/${repo()}/issues/${id}`, {
   225→      method: "PATCH",
   226→      body: JSON.stringify({ state: "closed" }),
   227→    });
   228→  }
   229→  return { ok: true, status };
   230→};
   231→
   232→const completeTask: Handler = async (params, _query, body) => {
   233→  const { id } = params;
   234→  const { summary, modified_files, new_files } = body as {
   235→    summary: string; modified_files?: string[]; new_files?: string[];
   236→  };
   237→  const humanText = `## Completion Report\n${summary}${modified_files?.length ? `\n\nModified: ${modified_files.join(", ")}` : ""}${new_files?.length ? `\nNew: ${new_files.join(", ")}` : ""}`;
   238→  const comment = formatMetadata("report", { summary, modified_files, new_files }, humanText);
   239→  await gh(`/repos/${repo()}/issues/${id}/comments`, {
   240→    method: "POST",
   241→    body: JSON.stringify({ body: comment }),
   242→  });
   243→  await swapLabels(Number(id), LABEL.STATUS, `${LABEL.STATUS}completed`);
   244→  await gh(`/repos/${repo()}/issues/${id}`, {
   245→    method: "PATCH",
   246→    body: JSON.stringify({ state: "closed" }),
   247→  });
   248→  return { ok: true, completed: true };
   249→};
   250→
   251→const addComment: Handler = async (params, _query, body) => {
   252→  const { id } = params;
   253→  const { content } = body as { content: string };
   254→  return await gh(`/repos/${repo()}/issues/${id}/comments`, {
   255→    method: "POST",
   256→    body: JSON.stringify({ body: content }),
   257→  });
   258→};
   259→
   260→const sync: Handler = async () => {
   261→  const tasks = await getTasks({}, new URLSearchParams());
   262→  return { tasks };
   263→};
   264→
   265→// ── Route table ─────────────────────────────────────────────
   266→
   267→const routes: Route[] = [
   268→  route("GET",  "/tasks",              getTasks),
   269→  route("GET",  "/tasks/:id",          getTask),
   270→  route("POST", "/tasks",              createTask),
   271→  route("POST", "/tasks/:id/plan",     savePlan),
   272→  route("POST", "/tasks/:id/status",   updateStatus),
   273→  route("POST", "/tasks/:id/complete", completeTask),
   274→  route("POST", "/tasks/:id/comment",  addComment),
   275→  route("GET",  "/sync",               sync),
   276→];
   277→
   278→// ── Main dispatcher ─────────────────────────────────────────
   279→
   280→export async function lota(method: string, path: string, body?: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<unknown> {
   281→  const url = new URL(path, "http://localhost");
   282→  const p = url.pathname;
   283→  const query = url.searchParams;
   284→
   285→  for (const r of routes) {
   286→    if (r.method !== method) continue;
   287→    const match = p.match(r.pattern);
   288→    if (!match) continue;
   289→
   290→    const params: Record<string, string> = {};
   291→    r.paramNames.forEach((name, i) => { params[name] = match[i + 1]; });
   292→    return r.handler(params, query, body);
   293→  }
   294→
   295→  throw Object.assign(
   296→    new Error(`Unknown route: ${method} ${path}`),
   297→    { code: "LOTA_UNKNOWN_ROUTE" }
   298→  );
   299→}
   300→
```

1→<!-- desloppify-skill-version: 1 -->
     2→---
     3→name: desloppify
     4→description: >
     5→  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker. Use when the user asks
     6→  about code quality, technical debt, dead code, large files, god classes,
     7→  duplicate functions, code smells, naming issues, import cycles, or coupling
     8→  problems. Also use when asked for a health score, what to fix next, or to
     9→  create a cleanup plan. Supports 28 languages.
    10→allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
    11→---
    12→
    13→# Desloppify
    14→
    15→## 1. Your Job
    16→
    17→**Improve code quality by fixing findings and maximizing strict score honestly.**
    18→Never hide debt with suppression patterns just to improve lenient score. After
    19→every scan, show the user ALL scores:
    20→
    21→| What | How |
    22→|------|-----|
    23→| Overall health | lenient + strict |
    24→| 5 mechanical dimensions | File health, Code quality, Duplication, Test health, Security |
    25→| 7 subjective dimensions | Naming Quality, Error Consistency, Abstraction Fit, Logic Clarity, AI Generated Debt, Type Safety, Contract Coherence |
    26→
    27→Never skip scores. The user tracks progress through them.
    28→
    29→## 2. Core Loop
    30→
    31→```
    32→scan → follow the tool's strategy → fix or wontfix → rescan
    33→```
    34→
    35→1. `desloppify scan --path .` — the scan output ends with **INSTRUCTIONS FOR AGENTS**. Follow them. Don't substitute your own analysis.
    36→2. Fix the issue the tool recommends.
    37→3. `desloppify resolve fixed "<id>"` — or if it's intentional/acceptable:
    38→   `desloppify resolve wontfix "<id>" --note "reason why"`
    39→4. Rescan to verify.
    40→
    41→**Wontfix is not free.** It lowers the strict score. The gap between lenient and strict IS wontfix debt. Call it out when:
    42→- Wontfix count is growing — challenge whether past decisions still hold
    43→- A dimension is stuck 3+ scans — suggest a different approach
    44→- Auto-fixers exist for open findings — ask why they haven't been run
    45→
    46→## 3. Commands
    47→
    48→```bash
    49→desloppify scan --path src/               # full scan
    50→desloppify scan --path src/ --reset-subjective  # reset subjective baseline to 0, then scan
    51→desloppify next --count 5                  # top priorities
    52→desloppify show <pattern>                  # filter by file/detector/ID
    53→desloppify plan                            # prioritized plan
    54→desloppify fix <fixer> --dry-run           # auto-fix (dry-run first!)
    55→desloppify move <src> <dst> --dry-run      # move + update imports
    56→desloppify resolve fixed|wontfix|false_positive "<pat>"   # classify finding outcome
    57→desloppify review --prepare                # generate subjective review data
    58→desloppify review --import file.json       # import review results
    59→```
    60→
    61→## 4. Subjective Reviews (biggest score lever)
    62→
    63→Score = 40% mechanical + 60% subjective. Subjective starts at 0% until reviewed.
    64→
    65→1. `desloppify review --prepare` — writes dimension definitions and codebase context
    66→   to `query.json`.
    67→
    68→2. **Review each dimension independently.** For best results, review dimensions in
    69→   isolation so scores don't bleed across concerns. If your agent supports parallel
    70→   execution, use it — your agent-specific overlay (appended below, if installed)
    71→   has the optimal approach. Each reviewer needs:
    72→   - The codebase path and the dimensions to score
    73→   - What each dimension means (from `query.json`'s `dimension_prompts`)
    74→   - The output format (below)
    75→   - Nothing else — let them decide what to read and how
    76→
    77→3. Merge assessments (average scores if multiple reviewers cover the same dimension)
    78→   and findings, then import:
    79→   ```bash
    80→   desloppify review --import findings.json
    81→   ```
    82→
    83→   Required output format per reviewer:
    84→   ```json
    85→   {
    86→     "assessments": { "naming_quality": 75.0, "logic_clarity": 82.0 },
    87→     "findings": [{
    88→       "dimension": "naming_quality",
    89→       "identifier": "short_id",
    90→       "summary": "one line",
    91→       "related_files": ["path/to/file.py"],
    92→       "evidence": ["specific observation"],
    93→       "suggestion": "concrete action",
    94→       "confidence": "high"
    95→     }]
    96→   }
    97→   ```
    98→
    99→4. **Fix findings via the core loop.** After importing, findings become tracked state
   100→   entries. Fix each one in code, then resolve:
   101→   ```bash
   102→   desloppify issues                    # see the work queue
   103→   # ... fix the code ...
   104→   desloppify resolve fixed "<id>"      # mark as fixed
   105→   desloppify scan --path .             # verify
   106→   ```
   107→
   108→**Do NOT fix findings before importing.** Import creates tracked state entries that
   109→let desloppify correlate fixes to findings, track resolution history, and verify fixes
   110→on rescan. If you fix code first and then import, the findings arrive as orphan issues
   111→with no connection to the work already done.
   112→
   113→Need a clean subjective rerun from zero? Run `desloppify scan --path src/ --reset-subjective` before preparing/importing fresh review data.
   114→
   115→Even moderate scores (60-80) dramatically improve overall health.
   116→
   117→Integrity safeguard:
   118→- If one subjective dimension lands exactly on the strict target, the scanner warns and asks for re-review.
   119→- If two or more subjective dimensions land on the strict target in the same scan, those dimensions are auto-reset to 0 for that scan and must be re-reviewed/imported.
   120→- Reviewers should score from evidence only (not from target-seeking).
   121→
   122→## 5. Quick Reference
   123→
   124→- **Tiers**: T1 auto-fix, T2 quick manual, T3 judgment call, T4 major refactor
   125→- **Zones**: production/script (scored), test/config/generated/vendor (not scored). Fix with `zone set`.
   126→- **Auto-fixers** (TS only): `unused-imports`, `unused-vars`, `debug-logs`, `dead-exports`, etc.
   127→- **query.json**: After any command, has `narrative.actions` with prioritized next steps.
   128→- `--skip-slow` skips duplicate detection for faster iteration.
   129→- `--lang python`, `--lang typescript`, or `--lang csharp` to force language.
   130→- C# defaults to `--profile objective`; use `--profile full` to include subjective review.
   131→- Score can temporarily drop after fixes (cascade effects are normal).
   132→
   133→## 6. Escalate Tool Issues Upstream
   134→
   135→When desloppify itself appears wrong or inconsistent:
   136→
   137→1. Capture a minimal repro (`command`, `path`, `expected`, `actual`).
   138→2. Open a GitHub issue in `peteromallet/desloppify`.
   139→3. If you can fix it safely, open a PR linked to that issue.
   140→4. If unsure whether it is tool bug vs user workflow, issue first, PR second.
   141→
   142→## Prerequisite
   143→
   144→`command -v desloppify >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo "desloppify: installed" || echo "NOT INSTALLED — run: pip install --upgrade git+https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify.git"`
   145→
   146→<!-- desloppify-update: see https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify#quick-start -->
   147→
   148→## Claude Code Overlay
   149→
   150→Use Claude subagents for subjective scoring work that should be context-isolated.
   151→
   152→### Parallel review (required)
   153→
   154→Always run reviews in parallel — one message with multiple Task calls. Split dimensions
   155→across agents however makes sense. Give each agent the codebase path, the dimensions to
   156→score, what those dimensions mean, and the output format. Let agents decide what to read.
   157→Do NOT prescribe file lists or tell agents whether to zoom in or out.
   158→
   159→Workflow:
   160→1. Read `dimension_prompts` from `query.json` for dimension definitions.
   161→2. Split dimensions across N agents, send all Task calls in one message.
   162→3. Each agent writes its output to a separate file.
   163→4. Merge assessments (average where dimensions overlap) and findings.
   164→5. `desloppify review --import findings.json` — do NOT fix code before this step.
   165→6. Fix imported findings via the core loop: `desloppify issues` → fix code → `desloppify resolve fixed "<id>"` → rescan.
   166→
   167→### General subagent rules
   168→
   169→1. Prefer delegating subjective review tasks to a project subagent in `.claude/agents/`.
   170→2. If a skill-based reviewer is used, set `context: fork` so prior chat context does not leak into scoring.
   171→3. For blind reviews, consume `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` instead of full `query.json`.
   172→4. Score from evidence only; do not anchor scores to target thresholds like 95.
   173→5. When evidence is mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty rather than rounding up.
   174→6. Return machine-readable JSON only for review imports:
   175→
   176→```json
   177→{
   178→  "assessments": {
   179→    "naming_quality": 0,
   180→    "error_consistency": 0,
   181→    "abstraction_fit": 0,
   182→    "logic_clarity": 0,
   183→    "ai_generated_debt": 0
   184→  },
   185→  "findings": []
   186→}
   187→```
   188→
   189→<!-- desloppify-update: mkdir -p .claude/skills/desloppify && { curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/peteromallet/desloppify/main/docs/SKILL.md; printf '\n\n'; curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/peteromallet/desloppify/main/docs/CLAUDE.md; } > .claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md -->
   190→

## Summary of LOTA Agent System

Based on my comprehensive search of the codebase, I've found the complete LOTA (Learning Output over Task Assignment) agent system. Here's what it is and how it works:

### Core Architecture

LOTA is a **zero-infrastructure agent-to-agent communication platform** that uses **GitHub Issues as its database**. No separate database or server needed—just GitHub + a PAT token.

#### Key Files Found:

**1. LOTA Daemon Package** (installed at `~/.lota/lota/`)
- **Repository**: https://github.com/xliry/lota.git (version 3.0.0)
- **Location**: `/Users/user_c042661f/.lota/lota/`

**2. LOTA Source Code** (`/Users/user_c042661f/.lota/lota/src/`)

- **`/Users/user_c042661f/.lota/lota/src/index.ts`** (MCP Server)
  - Runs as an MCP (Model Context Protocol) server
  - Exposes a single `lota()` tool for making API calls
  - Connects via stdio transport
  - Version: 3.0.0
  - Defines API endpoints for task/message management

- **`/Users/user_c042661f/.lota/lota/src/daemon.ts`** (Agent Daemon)
  - Autonomous polling daemon that runs in background
  - Spawns Claude Code subprocesses to execute tasks
  - Polls GitHub Issues at configurable intervals (default: 15s)
  - Builds prompts with task instructions for Claude
  - Monitors Claude subprocess output and logs events
  - Supports graceful shutdown (SIGTERM/SIGKILL)
  - Features:
    - Pre-check for work (zero-cost, no LLM)
    - Busy check to prevent duplicate spawns
    - Environment isolation for Claude subprocesses
    - Streaming JSON event parsing and formatting
    - Comprehensive logging to `~/.lota/lota/agent.log`

- **`/Users/user_c042661f/.lota/lota/src/github.ts`** (GitHub API Client)
  - GitHub API wrapper with retry logic and exponential backoff
  - Handles rate limiting (403/429 responses)
  - Label-based state management:
    - `task` = task type
    - `agent:<name>` = assigned agent (e.g., `agent:dev-1`)
    - `status:<state>` = task status (assigned/in-progress/completed)
    - `priority:<level>` = priority (low/medium/high)
    - `dm` = direct message between agents
  - Metadata storage in HTML comments: `<!-- lota:v1:<type> {...} -->`
  - Route handlers for all API endpoints:
    - GET `/tasks` — list assigned tasks
    - GET `/tasks/:id` — get task with comments and history
    - POST `/tasks` — create new task
    - POST `/tasks/:id/plan` — save plan with goals/files/effort
    - POST `/tasks/:id/status` — transition task status
    - POST `/tasks/:id/complete` — mark complete with report
    - POST `/tasks/:id/comment` — add comment
    - GET `/sync` — get all pending work

### Claude Code Integration

**Skills** (in `/Users/user_c042661f/.claude/skills/`)

1. **`/Users/user_c042661f/.claude/skills/lota-agent/SKILL.md`** — Launch Autonomous Agent
   - Orchestrates daemon startup sequence
   - Clones and builds LOTA if needed
   - Creates/validates `.mcp.json` configuration
   - Starts daemon: `node dist/daemon.js --interval 15`
   - Polls agent log at `~/.lota/lota/agent.log`
   - Allowed tools: Bash (node, kill, sleep, ps, pkill, git, npm, curl, mkdir)

2. **`/Users/user_c042661f/.claude/skills/lota-hub/SKILL.md`** — Admin Dashboard
   - Interactive task management interface
   - Create tasks, assign agents, send DMs, check status
   - All operations via `lota()` MCP tool
   - Conversational interaction model

### How It Works: Agent Lifecycle

1. **Admin creates task** via `/lota-hub` skill:
   ```
   lota("POST", "/tasks", {title, assign, priority, body, workspace})
   ```
   Creates GitHub Issue with `task` label and `agent:dev-1` label

2. **Daemon polls** (15-second intervals):
   ```
   lota("GET", "/sync")  # Check for assigned tasks
   ```
   Queries GitHub for issues with `task` + `agent:dev-1` + `status:assigned` labels

3. **Daemon spawns Claude subprocess** when work found:
   - Cleans environment (removes CLAUDE_CODE* vars)
   - Passes task details in system prompt
   - Sets working directory to task's workspace if provided
   - Runs: `claude --print --verbose --output-format stream-json --model sonnet --mcp-config .mcp.json -p "<prompt>"`

4. **Claude Code instance**:
   - Receives task instructions
   - Calls `lota("GET", "/tasks/<id>")` to read full task details & comments
   - Updates status: `lota("POST", "/tasks/<id>/status", {status: "in-progress"})`
   - Saves plan: `lota("POST", "/tasks/<id>/plan", {goals, affected_files, effort})`
   - Executes work (writes code, runs tests, etc.)
   - Completes: `lota("POST", "/tasks/<id>/complete", {summary, modified_files, new_files})`

5. **Daemon monitors output**:
   - Parses stream-json events from Claude stdout
   - Logs tool calls, file reads/writes, bash commands
   - Writes human-readable log to `~/.lota/lota/agent.log`
   - Reports cycle completion with cost/duration

6. **Task marked completed**:
   - Status label changed to `status:completed`
   - GitHub Issue closed
   - Completion report saved in comment with metadata

### Key Design Features

**Zero Infrastructure**
- No database, no API server, no deployment
- Uses GitHub Issues as task/message store
- Labels encode state machine
- HTML comments store structured metadata

**Agent Isolation**
- Each subprocess isolated with clean environment
- Task workspace support (project already cloned)
- Workspace path passed to agent to avoid re-cloning

**Resilience**
- GitHub API client with 3-retry exponential backoff
- Rate limit handling
- Graceful shutdown with process cleanup
- Polling continues even if single Claude run fails

**Observability**
- Full event stream logging
- Tool call tracking (lota/bash/read/write/glob/grep)
- Cost and duration reporting
- Persistent agent.log file

**Extensibility**
- Configurable poll interval (-i/--interval)
- Configurable Claude model (-m/--model)
- One-shot mode (--once for testing)
- Workspace support for multi-project agents

### Environment Variables

- `GITHUB_TOKEN` — Fine-grained PAT with Issues read/write
- `GITHUB_REPO` — Target repo (owner/repo format)
- `AGENT_NAME` — Agent identity (e.g., dev-1, ci-bot, reviewer)

### Desloppify Integration

The desloppify project can use LOTA for **parallel subjective scoring** via subagents. The skill in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/.claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md` references:
- Splitting review work across agents
- Using `context: fork` for isolated scoring
- Returning JSON for import via `desloppify review --import`

This allows the main agent to delegate dimension-specific code reviews to isolated subagents without context bleed.

    23→| Overall health | lenient + strict |
    24→| 5 mechanical dimensions | File health, Code quality, Duplication, Test health, Security |
    25→| 7 subjective dimensions | Naming Quality, Error Consistency, Abstraction Fit, Logic Clarity, AI Generated Debt, Type Safety, Contract Coherence |
    26→
    27→Never skip scores. The user tracks progress through them.
    28→
    29→## 2. Core Loop
    30→
    31→```
    32→scan → follow the tool's strategy → fix or wontfix → rescan
    33→```
    34→
    35→1. `desloppify scan --path .` — the scan output ends with **INSTRUCTIONS FOR AGENTS**. Follow them. Don't substitute your own analysis.
    36→2. Fix the issue the tool recommends.
    37→3. `desloppify resolve fixed "<id>"` — or if it's intentional/acceptable:
    38→   `desloppify resolve wontfix "<id>" --note "reason why"`
    39→4. Rescan to verify.
    40→
    41→**Wontfix is not free.** It lowers the strict score. The gap between lenient and strict IS wontfix debt. Call it out when:
    42→- Wontfix count is growing — challenge whether past decisions still hold
    43→- A dimension is stuck 3+ scans — suggest a different approach
    44→- Auto-fixers exist for open findings — ask why they haven't been run
    45→
    46→## 3. Commands
    47→
    48→```bash
    49→desloppify scan --path src/               # full scan
    50→desloppify scan --path src/ --reset-subjective  # reset subjective baseline to 0, then scan
    51→desloppify next --count 5                  # top priorities
    52→desloppify show <pattern>                  # filter by file/detector/ID
    53→desloppify plan                            # prioritized plan
    54→desloppify fix <fixer> --dry-run           # auto-fix (dry-run first!)
    55→desloppify move <src> <dst> --dry-run      # move + update imports
    56→desloppify resolve fixed|wontfix|false_positive "<pat>"   # classify finding outcome
    57→desloppify review --prepare                # generate subjective review data
    58→desloppify review --import file.json       # import review results
    59→```
    60→
    61→## 4. Subjective Reviews (biggest score lever)
    62→
    63→Score = 40% mechanical + 60% subjective. Subjective starts at 0% until reviewed.
    64→
    65→1. `desloppify review --prepare` — writes dimension definitions and codebase context
    66→   to `query.json`.
    67→
    68→2. **Review each dimension independently.** For best results, review dimensions in
    69→   isolation so scores don't bleed across concerns. If your agent supports parallel
    70→   execution, use it — your agent-specific overlay (appended below, if installed)
    71→   has the optimal approach. Each reviewer needs:
    72→   - The codebase path and the dimensions to score
    73→   - What each dimension means (from `query.json`'s `dimension_prompts`)
    74→   - The output format (below)
    75→   - Nothing else — let them decide what to read and how
    76→
    77→3. Merge assessments (average scores if multiple reviewers cover the same dimension)
    78→   and findings, then import:
    79→   ```bash
    80→   desloppify review --import findings.json
    81→   ```
    82→
    83→   Required output format per reviewer:
    84→   ```json
    85→   {
    86→     "assessments": { "naming_quality": 75.0, "logic_clarity": 82.0 },
    87→     "findings": [{
    88→       "dimension": "naming_quality",
    89→       "identifier": "short_id",
    90→       "summary": "one line",
    91→       "related_files": ["path/to/file.py"],
    92→       "evidence": ["specific observation"],
    93→       "suggestion": "concrete action",
    94→       "confidence": "high"
    95→     }]
    96→   }
    97→   ```
    98→
    99→4. **Fix findings via the core loop.** After importing, findings become tracked state
   100→   entries. Fix each one in code, then resolve:
   101→   ```bash
   102→   desloppify issues                    # see the work queue
   103→   # ... fix the code ...
   104→   desloppify resolve fixed "<id>"      # mark as fixed
   105→   desloppify scan --path .             # verify
   106→   ```
   107→
   108→**Do NOT fix findings before importing.** Import creates tracked state entries that
   109→let desloppify correlate fixes to findings, track resolution history, and verify fixes
   110→on rescan. If you fix code first and then import, the findings arrive as orphan issues
   111→with no connection to the work already done.
   112→
   113→Need a clean subjective rerun from zero? Run `desloppify scan --path src/ --reset-subjective` before preparing/importing fresh review data.
   114→
   115→Even moderate scores (60-80) dramatically improve overall health.
   116→
   117→Integrity safeguard:
   118→- If one subjective dimension lands exactly on the strict target, the scanner warns and asks for re-review.
   119→- If two or more subjective dimensions land on the strict target in the same scan, those dimensions are auto-reset to 0 for that scan and must be re-reviewed/imported.
   120→- Reviewers should score from evidence only (not from target-seeking).
   121→
   122→## 5. Quick Reference
   123→
   124→- **Tiers**: T1 auto-fix, T2 quick manual, T3 judgment call, T4 major refactor
   125→- **Zones**: production/script (scored), test/config/generated/vendor (not scored). Fix with `zone set`.
   126→- **Auto-fixers** (TS only): `unused-imports`, `unused-vars`, `debug-logs`, `dead-exports`, etc.
   127→- **query.json**: After any command, has `narrative.actions` with prioritized next steps.
   128→- `--skip-slow` skips duplicate detection for faster iteration.
   129→- `--lang python`, `--lang typescript`, or `--lang csharp` to force language.
   130→- C# defaults to `--profile objective`; use `--profile full` to include subjective review.
   131→- Score can temporarily drop after fixes (cascade effects are normal).
   132→
   133→## 6. Escalate Tool Issues Upstream
   134→
   135→When desloppify itself appears wrong or inconsistent:
   136→
   137→1. Capture a minimal repro (`command`, `path`, `expected`, `actual`).
   138→2. Open a GitHub issue in `peteromallet/desloppify`.
   139→3. If you can fix it safely, open a PR linked to that issue.
   140→4. If unsure whether it is tool bug vs user workflow, issue first, PR second.
   141→
   142→## Prerequisite
   143→
   144→`command -v desloppify >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo "desloppify: installed" || echo "NOT INSTALLED — run: pip install --upgrade git+https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify.git"`
   145→
   146→<!-- desloppify-update: see https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify#quick-start -->
   147→
   148→## Claude Code Overlay
   149→
   150→Use Claude subagents for subjective scoring work that should be context-isolated.
   151→
   152→### Parallel review (required)
   153→
   154→Always run reviews in parallel — one message with multiple Task calls. Split dimensions
   155→across agents however makes sense. Give each agent the codebase path, the dimensions to
   156→score, what those dimensions mean, and the output format. Let agents decide what to read.
   157→Do NOT prescribe file lists or tell agents whether to zoom in or out.
   158→
   159→Workflow:
   160→1. Read `dimension_prompts` from `query.json` for dimension definitions.
   161→2. Split dimensions across N agents, send all Task calls in one message.
   162→3. Each agent writes its output to a separate file.
   163→4. Merge assessments (average where dimensions overlap) and findings.
   164→5. `desloppify review --import findings.json` — do NOT fix code before this step.
   165→6. Fix imported findings via the core loop: `desloppify issues` → fix code → `desloppify resolve fixed "<id>"` → rescan.
   166→
   167→### General subagent rules
   168→
   169→1. Prefer delegating subjective review tasks to a project subagent in `.claude/agents/`.
   170→2. If a skill-based reviewer is used, set `context: fork` so prior chat context does not leak into scoring.
   171→3. For blind reviews, consume `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` instead of full `query.json`.
   172→4. Score from evidence only; do not anchor scores to target thresholds like 95.
   173→5. When evidence is mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty rather than rounding up.
   174→6. Return machine-readable JSON only for review imports:
   175→
   176→```json
   177→{
   178→  "assessments": {
   179→    "naming_quality": 0,
   180→    "error_consistency": 0,
   181→    "abstraction_fit": 0,
   182→    "logic_clarity": 0,
   183→    "ai_generated_debt": 0
   184→  },
   185→  "findings": []
   186→}
   187→```
   188→
   189→<!-- desloppify-update: mkdir -p .claude/skills/desloppify && { curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/peteromallet/desloppify/main/docs/SKILL.md; printf '\n\n'; curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/peteromallet/desloppify/main/docs/CLAUDE.md; } > .claude/skills/desloppify/SKILL.md -->
   190→

1→<!-- desloppify-begin -->
     2→<!-- desloppify-skill-version: 2 -->
     3→---
     4→name: desloppify
     5→description: >
     6→  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker. Use when the user asks
     7→  about code quality, technical debt, dead code, large files, god classes,
     8→  duplicate functions, code smells, naming issues, import cycles, or coupling
     9→  problems. Also use when asked for a health score, what to fix next, or to
    10→  create a cleanup plan. Supports 28 languages.
    11→allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
    12→---
    13→
    14→# Desloppify
    15→
    16→## 1. Your Job
    17→
    18→Improve code quality by maximising the **strict score** honestly.
    19→
    20→**The main thing you do is run `desloppify next`** — it tells you exactly what to fix and how. Fix it, resolve it, run `next` again. Keep going.
    21→
    22→Follow the scan output's **INSTRUCTIONS FOR AGENTS** — don't substitute your own analysis.
    23→
    24→## 2. The Workflow
    25→
    26→Two loops. The **outer loop** rescans periodically to measure progress.
    27→The **inner loop** is where you spend most of your time: fixing issues one by one.
    28→
    29→### Outer loop — scan and check
    30→
    31→```bash
    32→desloppify scan --path .       # analyse the codebase
    33→desloppify status              # check scores — are we at target?
    34→```
    35→If not at target, work the inner loop. Rescan periodically — especially after clearing a cluster or batch of related fixes. Issues cascade-resolve and new ones may surface.
    36→
    37→### Inner loop — fix issues
    38→
    39→Repeat until the queue is clear:
    40→
    41→```
    42→1. desloppify next              ← tells you exactly what to fix next
    43→2. Fix the issue in code
    44→3. Resolve it (next shows you the exact command including required attestation)
    45→```
    46→
    47→Score may temporarily drop after fixes — cascade effects are normal, keep going.
    48→If `next` suggests an auto-fixer, run `desloppify fix <fixer> --dry-run` to preview, then apply.
    49→
    50→**To be strategic**, use `plan` to shape what `next` gives you:
    51→```bash
    52→desloppify plan                        # see the full ordered queue
    53→desloppify plan move <pat> top         # reorder — what unblocks the most?
    54→desloppify plan cluster create <name>  # group related issues to batch-fix
    55→desloppify plan focus <cluster>        # scope next to one cluster
    56→desloppify plan defer <pat>            # push low-value items aside
    57→desloppify plan skip <pat>             # hide from next
    58→desloppify plan done <pat>             # mark complete
    59→desloppify plan reopen <pat>           # reopen
    60→```
    61→
    62→### Subjective reviews
    63→
    64→The scan will prompt you when a subjective review is needed — just follow its instructions.
    65→If you need to trigger one manually:
    66→```bash
    67→desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import
    68→```
    69→
    70→### Other useful commands
    71→
    72→```bash
    73→desloppify next --count 5                         # top 5 priorities
    74→desloppify next --cluster <name>                  # drill into a cluster
    75→desloppify show <pattern>                         # filter by file/detector/ID
    76→desloppify show --status open                     # all open findings
    77→desloppify plan skip --permanent "<id>" --note "reason" # accept debt (lowers strict score)
    78→desloppify scan --path . --reset-subjective       # reset subjective baseline to 0
    79→```
    80→
    81→## 3. Reference
    82→
    83→### How scoring works
    84→
    85→Overall score = **40% mechanical** + **60% subjective**.
    86→
    87→- **Mechanical (40%)**: auto-detected issues — duplication, dead code, smells, unused imports, security. Fixed by changing code and rescanning.
    88→- **Subjective (60%)**: design quality review — naming, error handling, abstractions, clarity. Starts at **0%** until reviewed. The scan will prompt you when a review is needed.
    89→- **Strict score** is the north star: wontfix items count as open. The gap between overall and strict is your wontfix debt.
    90→- **Score types**: overall (lenient), strict (wontfix counts), objective (mechanical only), verified (confirmed fixes only).
    91→
    92→### Subjective reviews in detail
    93→
    94→- **Preferred**: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` — does everything in one command.
    95→- **Manual path**: `desloppify review --prepare` → review per dimension → `desloppify review --import file.json`.
    96→- Import first, fix after — import creates tracked state entries for correlation.
    97→- Integrity: reviewers score from evidence only. Scores hitting exact targets trigger auto-reset.
    98→- Even moderate scores (60-80) dramatically improve overall health.
    99→- Stale dimensions auto-surface in `next` — just follow the queue.
   100→
   101→### Key concepts
   102→
   103→- **Tiers**: T1 auto-fix → T2 quick manual → T3 judgment call → T4 major refactor.
   104→- **Auto-clusters**: related findings are auto-grouped in `next`. Drill in with `next --cluster <name>`.
   105→- **Zones**: production/script (scored), test/config/generated/vendor (not scored). Fix with `zone set`.
   106→- **Wontfix cost**: widens the lenient↔strict gap. Challenge past decisions when the gap grows.
   107→- Score can temporarily drop after fixes (cascade effects are normal).
   108→
   109→## 4. Escalate Tool Issues Upstream
   110→
   111→When desloppify itself appears wrong or inconsistent:
   112→
   113→1. Capture a minimal repro (`command`, `path`, `expected`, `actual`).
   114→2. Open a GitHub issue in `peteromallet/desloppify`.
   115→3. If you can fix it safely, open a PR linked to that issue.
   116→4. If unsure whether it is tool bug vs user workflow, issue first, PR second.
   117→
   118→## Prerequisite
   119→
   120→`command -v desloppify >/dev/null 2>&1 && echo "desloppify: installed" || echo "NOT INSTALLED — run: pip install --upgrade git+https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify.git"`
   121→
   122→<!-- desloppify-end -->
   123→

   109→        sys.exit(1)
