  1025→        for idx in indexes:
  1026→            queue_error = _emit_progress(
  1027→                progress_fn, idx, "queued", None,
  1028→                details={"max_workers": max_workers},
  1029→            )
  1030→            if queue_error is not None:
  1031→                _on_progress_error(idx, queue_error)
  1032→                failures.add(idx)
  1033→            futures[executor.submit(_run_one, idx)] = idx
  1034→
  1035→        pending = set(futures.keys())
  1036→
  1037→        if heartbeat is None:
  1038→            for future in as_completed(pending):
  1039→                _on_complete(future)
  1040→            return failures
  1041→
  1042→        while pending:
  1043→            try:
  1044→                future = next(as_completed(pending, timeout=heartbeat))
  1045→            except FuturesTimeoutError:
  1046→                _heartbeat(
  1047→                    pending,
  1048→                    futures,
  1049→                    started_at,
  1050→                    lock,
  1051→                    indexes,
  1052→                    progress_fn,
  1053→                    clock_fn,
  1054→                    error_log_fn=error_log_fn,
  1055→                )
  1056→                continue
  1057→            pending.discard(future)
  1058→            _on_complete(future)
  1059→    return sorted(failures)
  1060→
  1061→
  1062→def _heartbeat(
  1063→    pending,
  1064→    futures,
  1065→    started_at,
  1066→    lock,
  1067→    indexes,
  1068→    progress_fn,
  1069→    clock_fn,
  1070→    *,
  1071→    error_log_fn=None,
  1072→):
  1073→    """Build and emit a heartbeat with active/queued batch status."""
  1074→    with lock:
  1075→        active = sorted(futures[f] for f in pending if futures[f] in started_at)
  1076→    active_set = set(active)
  1077→    queued = sorted(futures[f] for f in pending if futures[f] not in active_set)
  1078→    elapsed = {
  1079→        idx: int(max(0.0, clock_fn() - started_at.get(idx, clock_fn())))
  1080→        for idx in active
  1081→    }
  1082→    heartbeat_error = _emit_progress(
  1083→        progress_fn, -1, "heartbeat", None,
  1084→        details={
  1085→            "active_batches": active,
  1086→            "queued_batches": queued,
  1087→            "elapsed_seconds": elapsed,
  1088→            "active_count": len(active),
  1089→            "queued_count": len(queued),
  1090→            "total_count": len(indexes),
  1091→        },
  1092→    )
  1093→    if heartbeat_error is not None and callable(error_log_fn):
  1094→        try:
  1095→            error_log_fn(-1, heartbeat_error)
  1096→        except Exception:
  1097→            pass  # error-logging callback failed; don't crash the batch
  1098→
  1099→
  1100→def _extract_payload_from_log(
  1101→    batch_index: int, raw_path: Path, extract_fn,
  1102→) -> dict[str, object] | None:
  1103→    """Try to recover a batch payload from the runner log file."""
  1104→    log_path = raw_path.parent.parent / "logs" / f"batch-{batch_index + 1}.log"
  1105→    if not log_path.exists():
  1106→        return None
  1107→    try:
  1108→        log_text = log_path.read_text()
  1109→    except OSError:
  1110→        return None
  1111→
  1112→    # Prefer the final STDOUT section for the attempt; if that fails, scan the full log.
  1113→    stdout_marker = "\nSTDOUT:\n"
  1114→    stderr_marker = "\n\nSTDERR:\n"
  1115→    stdout_start = log_text.rfind(stdout_marker)
  1116→    if stdout_start == -1 and log_text.startswith("STDOUT:\n"):
  1117→        stdout_start = 0
  1118→        stdout_offset = len("STDOUT:\n")
  1119→    elif stdout_start >= 0:
  1120→        stdout_offset = len(stdout_marker)
  1121→    else:
  1122→        stdout_offset = 0
  1123→    if stdout_start >= 0:
  1124→        start_idx = stdout_start + stdout_offset
  1125→        stdout_end = log_text.find(stderr_marker, start_idx)
  1126→        stdout_text = (
  1127→            log_text[start_idx:] if stdout_end == -1 else log_text[start_idx:stdout_end]
  1128→        )
  1129→        payload = extract_fn(stdout_text)
  1130→        if payload is not None:
  1131→            return payload
  1132→        # If the batch log has a concrete STDOUT section but it contains no parseable
  1133→        # payload, do not fallback to parsing the whole log. Full logs include the
  1134→        # prompt template (often with JSON examples), which can generate misleading
  1135→        # decode errors and hide the true runner failure in STDERR.
  1136→        return None
  1137→
  1138→    return extract_fn(log_text)
  1139→
  1140→
  1141→def collect_batch_results(
  1142→    *,
  1143→    selected_indexes: list[int],
  1144→    failures: list[int],
  1145→    output_files: dict[int, Path],
  1146→    allowed_dims: set[str],
  1147→    extract_payload_fn,
  1148→    normalize_result_fn,
  1149→) -> tuple[list[BatchResult], list[int]]:
  1150→    """Parse and normalize batch outputs, preserving prior failures."""
  1151→    batch_results: list[BatchResult] = []
  1152→    failure_set = set(failures)
  1153→    for idx in selected_indexes:
  1154→        had_execution_failure = idx in failure_set
  1155→        raw_path = output_files[idx]
  1156→        payload = None
  1157→        parsed_from_log = False
  1158→        if raw_path.exists():
  1159→            try:
  1160→                payload = extract_payload_fn(raw_path.read_text())
  1161→            except OSError:
  1162→                payload = None
  1163→        if payload is None:
  1164→            payload = _extract_payload_from_log(idx, raw_path, extract_payload_fn)
  1165→            parsed_from_log = payload is not None
  1166→        if payload is None:
  1167→            failure_set.add(idx)
  1168→            continue
  1169→        if parsed_from_log:
  1170→            try:
  1171→                raw_path.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
  1172→            except OSError:
  1173→                pass
  1174→        try:
  1175→            assessments, findings, dimension_notes, quality = normalize_result_fn(
  1176→                payload,
  1177→                allowed_dims,
  1178→            )
  1179→        except ValueError:
  1180→            failure_set.add(idx)
  1181→            continue
  1182→        if had_execution_failure:
  1183→            failure_set.discard(idx)
  1184→        batch_results.append(
  1185→            BatchResult(
  1186→                batch_index=idx + 1,
  1187→                assessments=assessments,
  1188→                dimension_notes=dimension_notes,
  1189→                findings=findings,
  1190→                quality=quality,
  1191→            )
  1192→        )
  1193→    return batch_results, sorted(failure_set)
  1194→
  1195→
  1196→def _classify_runner_failure(log_text: str) -> str:
  1197→    """Classify batch failure type from log contents."""
  1198→    text = log_text.lower()
  1199→    if "timeout after" in text:
  1200→        return "timeout"
  1201→    if any(phrase in text for phrase in _USAGE_LIMIT_PHRASES):
  1202→        return "usage_limit"
  1203→    if any(phrase in text for phrase in _TRANSIENT_RUNNER_PHRASES):
  1204→        return "stream_disconnect"
  1205→    if (
  1206→        "codex not found" in text
  1207→        or ("no such file or directory" in text and "$ codex " in text)
  1208→        or ("errno 2" in text and "codex" in text)
  1209→    ):
  1210→        return "runner_missing"
  1211→    if any(
  1212→        phrase in text
  1213→        for phrase in (
  1214→            "not authenticated",
  1215→            "authentication failed",
  1216→            "unauthorized",
  1217→            "forbidden",
  1218→            "login required",
  1219→            "please login",
  1220→            "access token",
  1221→        )
  1222→    ):
  1223→        return "runner_auth"
  1224→    if "runner exception" in text:
  1225→        return "runner_exception"
  1226→    return "unknown"
  1227→
  1228→
  1229→def _has_codex_backend_connectivity_issue(log_text: str) -> bool:
  1230→    """Return True when logs indicate Codex backend URL is unreachable."""
  1231→    text = log_text.lower()
  1232→    if "error sending request for url" not in text:
  1233→        return False
  1234→    return (
  1235→        _CODEX_BACKEND_PATH_HINT in text
  1236→        or _CODEX_BACKEND_HOST_HINT in text
  1237→        or "nodename nor servname provided" in text
  1238→        or "name or service not known" in text
  1239→        or "temporary failure in name resolution" in text
  1240→    )
  1241→
  1242→
  1243→def _looks_like_restricted_sandbox(log_text: str) -> bool:
  1244→    """Return True when logs resemble a constrained agent sandbox execution."""
  1245→    text = log_text.lower()
  1246→    return any(phrase in text for phrase in _SANDBOX_PATH_WARNING_PHRASES)
  1247→
  1248→
  1249→def _summarize_failure_categories(*, failures: list[int], logs_dir: Path) -> dict[str, int]:
  1250→    """Return counts by failure category for failed batches."""
  1251→    categories: dict[str, int] = {}
  1252→    for idx in sorted(set(failures)):
  1253→        log_file = logs_dir / f"batch-{idx + 1}.log"
  1254→        if not log_file.exists():
  1255→            category = "missing_log"
  1256→        else:
  1257→            try:
  1258→                category = _classify_runner_failure(log_file.read_text())
  1259→            except OSError:
  1260→                category = "log_read_error"
  1261→        categories[category] = categories.get(category, 0) + 1
  1262→    return categories
  1263→
  1264→

  1267→    hints: list[str] = []

1→## Claude Code Overlay
     2→
     3→Use Claude subagents for subjective scoring work that should be context-isolated.
     4→
     5→### Subjective review
     6→
     7→1. **Preferred**: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` — does everything in one command.
     8→2. **Claude cloud path**: `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` → use generated `claude_launch_prompt.md` + `review_result.template.json` → run printed `desloppify review --external-submit --session-id <id> --import <file>`.
     9→3. **Manual path**: split dimensions across N subagents (one message, multiple Task calls), merge outputs, then `desloppify review --import findings.json`.
    10→
    11→For the manual path:
    12→- Read `dimension_prompts` from `query.json` for dimension definitions.
    13→- Give each agent the codebase path, dimensions, and output format. Let agents decide what to read.
    14→- Each agent writes output to a separate file. Merge assessments (average overlaps) and findings.
    15→- Import first, fix after — import creates tracked state for correlation.
    16→
    17→### Subagent rules
    18→
    19→1. Prefer delegating review tasks to a project subagent in `.claude/agents/`.
    20→2. Set `context: fork` so prior chat context does not leak into scoring.
    21→3. For blind reviews, consume `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` instead of full `query.json`.
    22→4. Score from evidence only; do not anchor to target thresholds. When mixed, score lower.
    23→5. Return machine-readable JSON matching the format in the base skill doc. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template.
    24→6. `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly. Use `"findings": []` when no defects found.
    25→7. Import is fail-closed: invalid findings abort unless `--allow-partial` is passed.
    26→
    27→<!-- desloppify-overlay: claude -->
    28→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    29→

    99→def _render_historical_focus(batch: dict[str, object]) -> str:
   100→    focus = batch.get("historical_issue_focus")
   101→    if not isinstance(focus, dict):
   102→        return ""
   103→
   104→    selected_raw = focus.get("selected_count", 0)
   105→    try:
   106→        selected_count = max(0, int(selected_raw))
   107→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   108→        selected_count = 0
   109→
   110→    issues = focus.get("issues", [])
   111→    if not isinstance(issues, list):
   112→        issues = []
   113→
   114→    if selected_count <= 0 or not issues:
   115→        return ""
   116→
   117→    lines: list[str] = []
   118→    lines.append(
   119→        "Previously flagged issues (from past reviews of these dimensions):"
   120→    )
   121→    lines.append(
   122→        "Check whether each issue still exists in the current code. Do not re-report"
   123→        " issues that have been fixed or marked wontfix — focus on what remains or"
   124→        " what is new. If several past issues share a root cause, call that out."
   125→    )
   126→
   127→    for entry in issues:
   128→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   129→            continue
   130→        status = str(entry.get("status", "")).strip()
   131→        summary = str(entry.get("summary", "")).strip()
   132→        note = str(entry.get("note", "")).strip()
   133→
   134→        line = f"  - [{status}] {summary}"
   135→        if note:
   136→            line += f" (note: {note})"
   137→        lines.append(line)
   138→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n\n"
   139→
   140→
   141→def _render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch: dict[str, object]) -> str:
   142→    """Render mechanically-generated concern hypotheses for this batch."""
   143→    signals = batch.get("concern_signals")
   144→    if not isinstance(signals, list) or not signals:
   145→        return ""
   146→
   147→    lines: list[str] = []
   148→    lines.append("Mechanical concern signals (detector synthesis hypotheses):")
   149→    lines.append(
   150→        "Treat each as a hypothesis: confirm or refute with direct code evidence."
   151→    )
   152→
   153→    shown = 0
   154→    for entry in signals:
   155→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   156→            continue
   157→        file = str(entry.get("file", "")).strip() or "(unknown file)"
   158→        concern_type = str(entry.get("type", "")).strip() or "design_concern"
   159→        summary = str(entry.get("summary", "")).strip()
   160→        question = str(entry.get("question", "")).strip()
   161→        evidence_raw = entry.get("evidence", [])
   162→        evidence = (
   163→            [str(item).strip() for item in evidence_raw if isinstance(item, str) and item.strip()]
   164→            if isinstance(evidence_raw, list)
   165→            else []
   166→        )
   167→
   168→        lines.append(f"  - [{concern_type}] {file}")
   169→        if summary:
   170→            lines.append(f"    summary: {summary}")
   171→        if question:
   172→            lines.append(f"    question: {question}")
   173→        for snippet in evidence[:2]:
   174→            lines.append(f"    evidence: {snippet}")
   175→        shown += 1
   176→        if shown >= 8:
   177→            break
   178→
   179→    extra = max(0, len(signals) - shown)
   180→    if extra:
   181→        lines.append(f"  - (+{extra} more concern signals)")
   182→    return "\n".join(lines) + "\n\n"

   219→        "flagged as overloaded, fragmented, or thin-wrapper patterns.\n"
   220→    )
   221→
   222→
   223→def _render_abstraction_focus(dim_set: set[str]) -> str:
   224→    if "abstraction_fitness" not in dim_set:
   225→        return ""
   226→    return (
   227→        "9d. For abstraction_fitness, use evidence from `holistic_context.abstractions`:\n"
   228→        "  - `delegation_heavy_classes`: classes where most methods forward to an inner "
   229→        "object — entries include class_name, delegate_target, sample_methods, and line number.\n"
   230→        "  - `facade_modules`: re-export-only modules with high re_export_ratio — entries "
   231→        "include samples (re-exported names) and loc.\n"
   232→        "  - `typed_dict_violations`: TypedDict fields accessed via .get()/.setdefault()/.pop() "
   233→        "— entries include typed_dict_name, violation_type, field, and line number.\n"
   234→        "  - `complexity_hotspots`: files where mechanical analysis found extreme parameter "
   235→        "counts, deep nesting, or disconnected responsibility clusters.\n"
   236→        "  Include `delegation_density`, `definition_directness`, and `type_discipline` "
   237→        "alongside existing sub-axes in dimension_notes when evidence supports it.\n"
   238→    )
   239→
   240→
   241→def _render_dimension_focus(context: _PromptBatchContext) -> str:
   242→    dim_set = context.dimension_set
   243→    return (
   244→        _render_package_org_focus(dim_set)
   245→        + _render_abstraction_focus(dim_set)
   246→        + _render_scan_evidence_focus(dim_set)
   247→        + _render_workflow_integrity_focus(dim_set)
   248→    )
   249→
   250→
   251→def _render_metadata_block(
   252→    *,
   253→    repo_root: Path,
   254→    packet_path: Path,
   255→    batch_index: int,
   256→    context: _PromptBatchContext,
   257→) -> str:
   258→    return (
   259→        "You are a focused subagent reviewer for a single holistic investigation batch.\n\n"
   260→        f"Repository root: {repo_root}\n"
   261→        f"Blind packet: {packet_path}\n"
   262→        f"Batch index: {batch_index + 1}\n"
   263→        f"Batch name: {context.name}\n"
   264→        f"Batch dimensions: {context.dimensions_text}\n"
   265→        f"Batch rationale: {context.rationale}\n\n"
   266→    )
   267→
   268→
   269→def _render_scan_evidence_note() -> str:
   270→    return (
   271→        "Mechanical scan evidence: The blind packet contains `holistic_context.scan_evidence` "
   272→        "with aggregated signals from all mechanical detectors — including complexity hotspots, "
   273→        "error hotspots, signal density index (files flagged by multiple detectors), boundary "
   274→        "violations, and systemic patterns. Consult this section for investigative leads beyond "
   275→        "the seed files.\n\n"
   276→    )
   277→
   278→
   279→def _render_seed_files_block(context: _PromptBatchContext) -> str:
   280→    return f"Seed files (start here):\n{context.seed_files_text}\n\n"
   281→
   282→
   283→def _render_task_requirements(context: _PromptBatchContext) -> str:
   284→    return (
   285→        "Task requirements:\n"
   286→        "1. Read the blind packet and follow `system_prompt` constraints exactly.\n"
   287→        "1a. If previously flagged issues are listed above, use them as context for your review.\n"
   288→        "    Verify whether each still applies to the current code. Do not re-report fixed or\n"
   289→        "    wontfix issues. Use them as starting points to look deeper — inspect adjacent code\n"
   290→        "    and related modules for defects the prior review may have missed.\n"
   291→        "1b. If mechanical concern signals are listed above, explicitly confirm or refute them.\n"
   292→        "    Report confirmed defects under the most impacted batch dimension.\n"
   293→        "    If refuting, include clear counter-evidence in `dimension_notes`.\n"
   294→        "1c. Think structurally: when you spot multiple individual issues that share a common\n"
   295→        "    root cause (missing abstraction, duplicated pattern, inconsistent convention),\n"
   296→        "    explain the deeper structural issue in the finding, not just the surface symptom.\n"
   297→        "    If the pattern is significant enough, report the structural issue as its own finding\n"
   298→        "    with appropriate fix_scope ('multi_file_refactor' or 'architectural_change') and\n"
   299→        "    use `root_cause_cluster` to connect related symptom findings together.\n"
   300→        "2. Start with the seed files, then freely explore additional repository files likely to surface material issues.\n"
   301→        "2a. Prioritize high-signal leads: unexplored/lightly reviewed files, historical issue areas, and hotspot neighbors "
   302→        "(high coupling, god modules, large files, churn seams).\n"
   303→        "2b. Keep exploration targeted — follow strongest evidence paths first instead of attempting exhaustive coverage.\n"
   304→        "2c. Keep findings and scoring scoped to this batch's listed dimensions.\n"
   305→        "2d. Respect scope controls in the blind packet config: do not include files/directories marked by "
   306→        "`exclude`, `ignore`, or zone overrides that classify files as non-production (test/config/generated/vendor).\n"
   307→        f"3. Return 0-{context.findings_cap} high-quality findings for this batch (empty array allowed).\n"
   308→        "3a. Do not suppress real defects to keep scores high; report every material issue you can support with evidence.\n"
   309→        "3b. Do not default to 100. Reserve 100 for genuinely exemplary evidence in this batch.\n"
   310→        "4. Score/finding consistency is required: broader or more severe findings MUST lower dimension scores.\n"
   311→        f"4a. Any dimension scored below {LOW_SCORE_FINDING_THRESHOLD:.1f} MUST include explicit feedback: add at least one "
   312→        "finding with the same `dimension` and a non-empty actionable `suggestion`.\n"
   313→        "5. Every finding must include `related_files` with at least 2 files when possible.\n"
   314→        "6. Every finding must include `dimension`, `identifier`, `summary`, `evidence`, `suggestion`, and `confidence`.\n"
   315→        "7. Every finding must include `impact_scope` and `fix_scope`.\n"
   316→        "8. Every scored dimension MUST include dimension_notes with concrete evidence.\n"
   317→        f"9. If a dimension score is >{HIGH_SCORE_ISSUES_NOTE_THRESHOLD:.1f}, include `{DIMENSION_NOTE_ISSUES_KEY}` in dimension_notes.\n"
   318→        "10. Use exactly one decimal place for every assessment and abstraction sub-axis score.\n"
   319→        f"{_render_dimension_focus(context)}"
   320→        "11. Ignore prior chat context and any target-threshold assumptions.\n"
   321→        "12. Do not edit repository files.\n"
   322→        "13. Return ONLY valid JSON, no markdown fences.\n\n"
   323→    )
   324→
   325→
   326→def _render_scope_enums() -> str:
   327→    return (
   328→        "Scope enums:\n"
   329→        '- impact_scope: "local" | "module" | "subsystem" | "codebase"\n'
   330→        '- fix_scope: "single_edit" | "multi_file_refactor" | "architectural_change"\n\n'
   331→    )
   332→
   333→
   334→def _render_output_schema(context: _PromptBatchContext, batch_index: int) -> str:
   335→    return (
   336→        "Output schema:\n"
   337→        "{\n"
   338→        f'  "batch": "{context.name}",\n'
   339→        f'  "batch_index": {batch_index + 1},\n'
   340→        '  "assessments": {"<dimension>": <0-100 with one decimal place>},\n'
   341→        '  "dimension_notes": {\n'
   342→        '    "<dimension>": {\n'
   343→        '      "evidence": ["specific code observations"],\n'
   344→        '      "impact_scope": "local|module|subsystem|codebase",\n'
   345→        '      "fix_scope": "single_edit|multi_file_refactor|architectural_change",\n'
   346→        '      "confidence": "high|medium|low",\n'
   347→        f'      "{DIMENSION_NOTE_ISSUES_KEY}": "required when score >{HIGH_SCORE_ISSUES_NOTE_THRESHOLD:.1f}",\n'
   348→        '      "sub_axes": {"abstraction_leverage": 0-100, "indirection_cost": 0-100, "interface_honesty": 0-100, "delegation_density": 0-100, "definition_directness": 0-100, "type_discipline": 0-100}  // required for abstraction_fitness when evidence supports it; all one decimal place\n'
   349→        "    }\n"
   350→        "  },\n"
   351→        '  "findings": [{\n'
   352→        '    "dimension": "<dimension>",\n'
   353→        '    "identifier": "short_id",\n'
   354→        '    "summary": "one-line defect summary",\n'
   355→        '    "related_files": ["relative/path.py"],\n'
   356→        '    "evidence": ["specific code observation"],\n'
   357→        '    "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",\n'
   358→        '    "confidence": "high|medium|low",\n'
   359→        '    "impact_scope": "local|module|subsystem|codebase",\n'
   360→        '    "fix_scope": "single_edit|multi_file_refactor|architectural_change",\n'
   361→        '    "root_cause_cluster": "optional_cluster_name_when_supported_by_history"\n'
   362→        "  }],\n"
   363→        '  "retrospective": {\n'
   364→        '    "root_causes": ["optional: concise root-cause hypotheses"],\n'
   365→        '    "likely_symptoms": ["optional: identifiers that look symptom-level"],\n'
   366→        '    "possible_false_positives": ["optional: prior concept keys likely mis-scoped"]\n'
   367→        "  }\n"
   368→        "}\n"
   369→    )
   370→
   371→
   372→def _join_non_empty_sections(*sections: str) -> str:
   373→    return "".join(section for section in sections if section)
   374→
   375→
   376→def render_batch_prompt(
   377→    *,
   378→    repo_root: Path,
   379→    packet_path: Path,
   380→    batch_index: int,
   381→    batch: dict[str, object],
   382→) -> str:
   383→    """Render one subagent prompt for a holistic investigation batch."""
   384→    context = _build_context(batch, batch_index)
   385→    return _join_non_empty_sections(
   386→        _render_metadata_block(
   387→            repo_root=repo_root,
   388→            packet_path=packet_path,
   389→            batch_index=batch_index,
   390→            context=context,
   391→        ),
   392→        _render_scan_evidence_note(),
   393→        _render_seed_files_block(context),
   394→        _render_historical_focus(batch),
   395→        _render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch),
   396→        _render_task_requirements(context),
   397→        _render_scope_enums(),
   398→        _render_output_schema(context, batch_index),
   399→    )
   400→
   401→
   402→__all__ = ["render_batch_prompt"]
   403→
```

1→## VS Code Copilot Overlay
     2→
     3→VS Code Copilot supports native subagents via `.github/agents/` definitions.
     4→Use them for context-isolated subjective reviews.
     5→
     6→### Subjective review
     7→
     8→1. **Preferred**: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.
     9→2. **Copilot/cloud path**: `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` → use generated prompt/template → run printed `--external-submit` command.
    10→3. **Manual path**: define a reviewer agent, split dimensions, merge, import.
    11→
    12→For the manual path, define a reviewer in `.github/agents/desloppify-reviewer.md`:
    13→
    14→```yaml
    15→---
    16→name: desloppify-reviewer
    17→tools: ['read', 'search']
    18→---
    19→You are a code quality reviewer. You will be given a codebase path, a set of
    20→dimensions to score, and what each dimension means. Read the code, score each
    21→dimension 0-100 from evidence only, and return JSON in the required format.
    22→Do not anchor to target thresholds. When evidence is mixed, score lower and
    23→explain uncertainty.
    24→```
    25→
    26→And an orchestrator in `.github/agents/desloppify-review-orchestrator.md`:
    27→
    28→```yaml
    29→---
    30→name: desloppify-review-orchestrator
    31→tools: ['agent', 'read', 'search']
    32→agents: ['desloppify-reviewer']
    33→---
    34→```
    35→
    36→Split dimensions across `desloppify-reviewer` calls (Copilot runs them concurrently), merge assessments (average overlaps) and findings, then import.
    37→
    38→### Review integrity
    39→
    40→1. Do not use prior chat context, score history, or target-threshold anchoring while scoring.
    41→2. Score from evidence only; when mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty.
    42→3. Return JSON matching the format in the base skill doc. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template.
    43→4. `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly. Use `"findings": []` when no defects found.
    44→5. Import is fail-closed: invalid findings abort unless `--allow-partial` is passed.
    45→
    46→<!-- desloppify-overlay: copilot -->
    47→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    48→
```

1→## Cursor Overlay
     2→
     3→Cursor supports native subagents via `.cursor/agents/` definitions. Use them
     4→for context-isolated subjective reviews.
     5→
     6→### Parallel review
     7→
     8→Split dimensions across subagents so each reviewer scores independently.
     9→Define a reviewer agent in `.cursor/agents/desloppify-reviewer.md`:
    10→
    11→```markdown
    12→---
    13→name: desloppify-reviewer
    14→description: Scores subjective codebase quality dimensions for desloppify
    15→tools:
    16→  - read
    17→  - search
    18→---
    19→
    20→You are a code quality reviewer. You will be given a codebase path, a set of
    21→dimensions to score, and what each dimension means. Read the code, score each
    22→dimension 0-100 from evidence only, and return JSON in the required format.
    23→Do not anchor to target thresholds. When evidence is mixed, score lower and
    24→explain uncertainty.
    25→```
    26→
    27→Workflow:
    28→1. Read `dimension_prompts` from `query.json` for dimension definitions.
    29→2. Launch multiple reviewer subagents, each with a subset of dimensions.
    30→3. Each agent writes its output to a separate file.
    31→4. Merge assessments (average where dimensions overlap) and findings.
    32→5. Preferred local path (Codex runner): `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.
    33→6. Cursor/cloud/manual path:
    34→   - robust session flow (recommended): `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude`; use generated `claude_launch_prompt.md` + `review_result.template.json`, then run printed `desloppify review --external-submit --session-id <id> --import <file>`
    35→   - durable scored import (legacy): `desloppify review --import findings.json --attested-external --attest "I validated this review was completed without awareness of overall score and is unbiased."`
    36→   - findings-only fallback: `desloppify review --import findings.json`
    37→
    38→### Review integrity
    39→
    40→1. Do not use prior chat context, score history, or target-threshold anchoring while scoring.
    41→2. Score from evidence only; when evidence is mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty.
    42→3. Return machine-readable JSON only for review imports. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template:
    43→
    44→```json
    45→{
    46→  "session": {
    47→    "id": "<session_id_from_template>",
    48→    "token": "<session_token_from_template>"
    49→  },
    50→  "assessments": {
    51→    "naming_quality": 0,
    52→    "error_consistency": 0,
    53→    "abstraction_fit": 0,
    54→    "logic_clarity": 0,
    55→    "ai_generated_debt": 0
    56→  },
    57→  "findings": [
    58→    {
    59→      "dimension": "naming_quality",
    60→      "identifier": "short_id",
    61→      "summary": "one-line defect summary",
    62→      "related_files": ["relative/path/to/file.py"],
    63→      "evidence": ["specific code observation"],
    64→      "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",
    65→      "confidence": "high|medium|low"
    66→    }
    67→  ]
    68→}
    69→```
    70→4. `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly. Use `"findings": []` only when no defects are found.
    71→5. Import is fail-closed by default: invalid/skipped findings abort `desloppify review --import` unless `--allow-partial` is explicitly passed.
    72→6. Assessment scores are auto-applied from trusted internal run-batches imports, or via Claude cloud session imports (`--external-start` + printed `--external-submit`). Legacy attested external import via `--attested-external` remains supported.
    73→
    74→<!-- desloppify-overlay: cursor -->
    75→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    76→
```

1→## OpenCode Overlay

     4→
     5→### Subjective review
     6→
     7→1. **Preferred**: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.
     8→2. **Manual path**: `desloppify review --prepare` → delegate to subagent for isolated scoring → `desloppify review --import findings.json`.

```
1→## Gemini CLI Overlay
     2→
     3→Gemini CLI has experimental subagent support, but subagents currently run
     4→sequentially (not in parallel). Review dimensions one at a time.
     5→
     6→### Setup
     7→
     8→Enable subagents in Gemini CLI settings:
     9→```json
    10→{
    11→  "experimental": {
    12→    "enableAgents": true
    13→  }
    14→}
    15→```
    16→
    17→Optionally define a reviewer agent in `.gemini/agents/desloppify-reviewer.md`:
    18→
    19→```yaml
    20→---
    21→name: desloppify-reviewer
    22→description: Scores subjective codebase quality dimensions for desloppify
    23→kind: local
    24→tools:
    25→  - read_file
    26→  - search_code
    27→temperature: 0.2
    28→max_turns: 10
    29→---
    30→
    31→You are a code quality reviewer. You will be given a codebase path, a set of
    32→dimensions to score, and what each dimension means. Read the code, score each
    33→dimension 0-100 from evidence only, and return JSON in the required format.
    34→Do not anchor to target thresholds. When evidence is mixed, score lower and
    35→explain uncertainty.
    36→```
    37→
    38→### Review workflow
    39→
    40→1. Preferred local path (Codex runner): `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.
    41→2. Gemini/cloud path: run `desloppify review --prepare` to generate `query.json`.
    42→3. Invoke the reviewer agent for each group of dimensions sequentially.
    43→   Even without parallelism, isolating dimensions across separate agent
    44→   invocations prevents score bleed between concerns.
    45→4. Merge assessments and findings, then import:
    46→   - robust session flow (recommended): `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude`; use generated `claude_launch_prompt.md` + `review_result.template.json`, then run printed `desloppify review --external-submit --session-id <id> --import <file>`
    47→   - durable scored import (legacy): `desloppify review --import findings.json --attested-external --attest "I validated this review was completed without awareness of overall score and is unbiased."`
    48→   - findings-only fallback: `desloppify review --import findings.json`
    49→
    50→When Gemini CLI adds parallel subagent execution, split dimensions across
    51→concurrent agent calls instead.
    52→
    53→### Review integrity
    54→
    55→1. Do not use prior chat context, score history, or target-threshold anchoring while scoring.
    56→2. Score from evidence only; when evidence is mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty.
    57→3. Return machine-readable JSON only for review imports. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template:
    58→
    59→```json
    60→{
    61→  "session": {
    62→    "id": "<session_id_from_template>",
    63→    "token": "<session_token_from_template>"
    64→  },
    65→  "assessments": {
    66→    "naming_quality": 0,
    67→    "error_consistency": 0,
    68→    "abstraction_fit": 0,
    69→    "logic_clarity": 0,
    70→    "ai_generated_debt": 0
    71→  },
    72→  "findings": [
    73→    {
    74→      "dimension": "naming_quality",
    75→      "identifier": "short_id",
    76→      "summary": "one-line defect summary",
    77→      "related_files": ["relative/path/to/file.py"],
    78→      "evidence": ["specific code observation"],
    79→      "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",
    80→      "confidence": "high|medium|low"
    81→    }
    82→  ]
    83→}
    84→```
    85→4. `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly. Use `"findings": []` only when no defects are found.
    86→5. Import is fail-closed by default: invalid/skipped findings abort `desloppify review --import` unless `--allow-partial` is explicitly passed.
    87→6. Assessment scores are auto-applied from trusted internal run-batches imports, or via Claude cloud session imports (`--external-start` + printed `--external-submit`). Legacy attested external import via `--attested-external` remains supported.
    88→
    89→<!-- desloppify-overlay: gemini -->
    90→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    91→
```

### KEY MECHANISMS

#### Parallel Scoring
- **ThreadPoolExecutor-based execution** with configurable worker count (default 3, max 8)
- **Per-batch timeouts** (default 20 min, configurable)
- **Heartbeat monitoring** every 15 seconds during parallel runs
- **Stall detection** and recovery: if output stable for threshold seconds with no stream activity, kill and recover from JSON
- **Retry logic** for transient failures (stream disconnects, network issues) with exponential backoff

#### Context Forking / Isolation
- **Blind packet generation** removes score hints, narrative, and target thresholds from packets sent to reviewers
- **Session tokens** used to validate reviewer outputs and track provenance
- **Explicit instructions** in prompts: "Ignore prior chat context and any target-threshold assumptions"
- **Separate output files** per batch to prevent cross-contamination
- **`context: fork`** directive in Claude Code documentation to prevent chat context leakage

#### Subagent Communication Patterns
- **Batch prompts** constructed with metadata blocks identifying the subagent, batch index, dimensions, and rationale
- **Pre-merged output** expected in specific JSON schema with assessments, findings, dimension_notes, and quality metrics
- **Provenance tracking** with runner type (codex/claude), run stamps, packet hashes, and batch indexes
- **Template outputs** provided to external reviewers with session IDs and tokens preserved

### EXTERNAL CLAUDE CLOUD WORKFLOW (External Session Management)

1. **Start Session**: `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude`
   - Creates session with ID, token, and expiration
   - Generates blind packet, template JSON, launch prompt, and instructions
   - All stored in `.desloppify/external_review_sessions/<session_id>/`

2. **Run in Isolated Subagent**: Copy/paste generated `claude_launch_prompt.md` into Claude subagent task with context isolation

3. **Submit Results**: `desloppify review --external-submit --session-id <id> --import <file>`
   - Validates session exists, not expired, has correct token
   - Adds canonical provenance metadata
   - Imports with `attested_external=True` and attestation text
   - Optionally runs follow-up scan

### DIRECTORY STRUCTURE FOR SUBAGENT RUNS

```
.desloppify/
├── subagents/runs/
│   └── <run_stamp>/
│       ├── prompts/
│       │   └── batch-<n>.md
│       ├── results/
│       │   └── batch-<n>.raw.txt
│       ├── logs/
│       │   └── batch-<n>.log
│       ├── run.log
│       └── run_summary.json
├── review_packets/
│   └── holistic_packet_<stamp>.json
├── review_packet_blind.json
└── external_review_sessions/
    └── <session_id>/
        ├── session.json
        ├── review_result.template.json
        ├── claude_launch_prompt.md
        ├── reviewer_instructions.md
        └── canonical_import_<stamp>.json
```

### SCORING PARAMETER CONTROLS

Available command-line flags in `batches.py` / `batch.py`:
- `--parallel`: Enable parallel execution
- `--max-parallel-batches`: Worker count (default 3, max varies)
- `--batch-timeout-seconds`: Per-batch timeout (default 1200s)
- `--batch-max-retries`: Retry count for transient failures (default 1)
- `--batch-retry-backoff-seconds`: Exponential backoff duration (default 2.0s)
- `--batch-heartbeat-seconds`: Progress heartbeat interval (default 15.0s)
- `--batch-stall-warning-seconds`: Elapsed time before stall warning
- `--batch-stall-kill-seconds`: Elapsed time before stall kill (default 120s)
- `--max-parallel-batches`: Concurrent workers when `--parallel` enabled (default 3, minimum 1)

1→---
     2→name: lota-hub
     3→description: >
     4→  LOTA admin dashboard. Create tasks, assign agents, send messages, check status,
     5→  and manage your agent workforce. Use when the user says "lota hub", "lota admin",
     6→  "send task", "check agents", "assign task", "create task", "manage agents",
     7→  or wants to manage agents and tasks.
     8→allowed-tools: mcp__lota__lota
     9→---
    10→
    11→# LOTA Hub — Admin Dashboard
    12→
    13→## What to do
    14→
    15→You are the LOTA admin interface. Help the user manage their agents interactively.
    16→
    17→### On launch, show the dashboard
    18→
    19→First, fetch current state:
    20→
    21→```
    22→lota("GET", "/sync")
    23→```
    24→
    25→Then display a summary:
    26→
    27→```
    28→LOTA Hub
    29→────────────────────────────
    30→  Tasks:    X pending, Y in-progress, Z completed
    31→  Messages: X unread
    32→────────────────────────────
    33→```
    34→
    35→### Available actions
    36→
    37→Ask the user what they want to do. Common actions:
    38→
    39→**Tasks**
    40→- Create task: `lota("POST", "/tasks", {"title": "...", "assign": "agent-name", "priority": "high|medium|low", "body": "..."})`
    41→- List tasks: `lota("GET", "/tasks")`
    42→- Check task: `lota("GET", "/tasks/<id>")`
    43→- Add comment: `lota("POST", "/tasks/<id>/comment", {"content": "..."})`
    44→
    45→**Messages**
    46→- Send DM: `lota("POST", "/messages", {"to": "agent-name", "content": "..."})`
    47→- Check messages: `lota("GET", "/messages")`
    48→- Reply: `lota("POST", "/messages/<id>/reply", {"content": "..."})`
    49→
    50→**Status**
    51→- Full sync: `lota("GET", "/sync")`
    52→- Filter tasks: `lota("GET", "/tasks?status=in-progress")`
    53→
    54→### Interaction style
    55→
    56→- Be conversational. Ask "What do you want to do?" after each action.
    57→- When creating tasks, ask for: title, agent to assign, priority, and description.
    58→- Show results in a clean, readable format.
    59→- Stay in hub mode — keep asking for next action until the user says they're done.
    60→
```

tool_result Read
```
1→---
     2→name: lota-agent
     3→description: >
     4→  Start the autonomous LOTA agent in the background. The agent polls GitHub Issues
     5→  for assigned tasks, then spawns Claude Code to plan, execute, and complete them.
     6→  Use when the user says "lota-agent", "start agent", "autonomous mode", "launch agent",
     7→  or wants to run the autonomous agent.
     8→allowed-tools: Bash(node *), Bash(cd * && node *), Bash(kill *), Bash(sleep *), Bash(ps *), Bash(pkill *), Bash(git clone *), Bash(npm *), Bash(curl *), Bash(mkdir *)
     9→---
    10→
    11→# LOTA Agent Skill
    12→
    13→## What to do
    14→
    15→Run this EXACT sequence. Do NOT run any other commands.
    16→
    17→### Step 1: Ensure lota is built
    18→
    19→Check if the build exists. If not, clone and build automatically:
    20→
    21→```bash
    22→if [ ! -f ~/.lota/lota/dist/daemon.js ]; then
    23→  git clone https://github.com/xliry/lota.git ~/.lota/lota && cd ~/.lota/lota && npm install && npm run build
    24→fi
    25→```
    26→
    27→If npm is not found, install Node.js first:
    28→```bash
    29→curl -fsSL https://deb.nodesource.com/setup_20.x | bash - && apt-get install -y nodejs
    30→```
    31→
    32→### Step 2: Ensure .mcp.json exists
    33→
    34→If `.mcp.json` does not exist in the current project directory, ask the user for:
    35→1. GitHub Token (fine-grained PAT with Issues read/write)
    36→2. GitHub Repo (owner/repo format)
    37→3. Agent Name (default: dev-1)
    38→
    39→Then write `.mcp.json` with those values. If it already exists, skip this step.
    40→
    41→### Step 3: Kill any existing agent
    42→
    43→```bash
    44→pkill -f "node.*daemon" 2>/dev/null; true
    45→```
    46→
    47→Note: This may show "exit code 144" — that's normal (process was killed). Ignore it.
    48→
    49→### Step 4: Start agent in background
    50→
    51→Run with `run_in_background: true` and `timeout: 600000`:
    52→
    53→```bash
    54→cd ~/.lota/lota && node dist/daemon.js --interval 15 2>&1
    55→```
    56→
    57→### Step 5: Wait and read log file
    58→
    59→```bash
    60→sleep 5
    61→```
    62→
    63→Then use the **Read** tool to read `~/.lota/agent.log`.
    64→
    65→### Step 6: Report to user
    66→
    67→Show the log content and tell the user:
    68→- The agent name (from banner)
    69→- The poll interval
    70→- "Agent is running. Check logs anytime: `cat ~/.lota/agent.log`"
    71→
    72→That's ALL. Do NOT run diagnostics, version checks, or anything else.
    73→
```

```
1→# lota
     2→
     3→Agent-to-agent communication over GitHub Issues. Zero infra — just a GitHub repo + PAT.
     4→
     5→## Get Started
     6→
     7→Paste this into your agent to set up LOTA automatically:
     8→
     9→```
    10→Set up LOTA — agent-to-agent communication over GitHub Issues. Zero infra, zero database.
    11→Tasks, messages, plans, and reports all live as GitHub Issues with labels.
    12→
    13→Step 1: Clone & build
    14→
    15→git clone https://github.com/xliry/lota.git ~/.lota/lota
    16→cd ~/.lota/lota && npm install && npm run build
    17→
    18→If ~/.lota/lota already exists: cd ~/.lota/lota && git pull && npm install && npm run build
    19→
    20→Requires Node.js 18+. If npm is not found, install Node.js first:
    21→curl -fsSL https://deb.nodesource.com/setup_20.x | bash - && apt-get install -y nodejs
    22→
    23→Step 2: Install skills
    24→
    25→Copy the LOTA skills into the current project so /lota-hub and /lota-agent work:
    26→
    27→mkdir -p .claude/skills/lota-agent .claude/skills/lota-hub
    28→cp ~/.lota/lota/.claude/skills/lota-agent/SKILL.md .claude/skills/lota-agent/SKILL.md
    29→cp ~/.lota/lota/.claude/skills/lota-hub/SKILL.md .claude/skills/lota-hub/SKILL.md
    30→cp ~/.lota/lota/.claude/settings.json .claude/settings.json
    31→
    32→Step 3: Configure
    33→
    34→Add to your project's .mcp.json (create if it doesn't exist):
    35→
    36→{
    37→  "mcpServers": {
    38→    "lota": {
    39→      "command": "node",
    40→      "args": ["~/.lota/lota/dist/index.js"],
    41→      "env": {
    42→        "GITHUB_TOKEN": "YOUR_GITHUB_PAT",
    43→        "GITHUB_REPO": "owner/repo-name",
    44→        "AGENT_NAME": "dev-1"
    45→      }
    46→    }
    47→  }
    48→}
    49→
    50→If .mcp.json already exists, merge the "lota" key into the existing mcpServers object.
    51→
    52→Ask the user for:
    53→1. GitHub Token — Fine-grained PAT with Issues read/write (Settings → Developer settings → Fine-grained tokens)
    54→2. GitHub Repo — A repo for agent communication (e.g. "username/my-agents"). If they don't have one, suggest creating a private repo.
    55→3. Agent Name — Their agent identity (default: "dev-1")
    56→
    57→Step 4: Restart & verify
    58→
    59→Tell the user to restart Claude Code so the MCP server loads and skills are recognized.
    60→After restart, test with:
    61→lota("GET", "/sync")
    62→
    63→Should return {"tasks": [], "messages": []} if the connection works.
    64→
    65→Step 5: Show what's possible
    66→
    67→Once connected, the user can:
    68→
    69→As Admin — create and assign tasks:
    70→  lota("POST", "/tasks", {"title": "Fix login bug", "assign": "dev-1", "priority": "high"})
    71→  lota("GET", "/tasks")
    72→  lota("POST", "/messages", {"to": "dev-1", "content": "Check the auth module"})
    73→
    74→As Agent — receive and complete tasks:
    75→  lota("GET", "/sync")
    76→  lota("POST", "/tasks/1/plan", {"goals": ["Investigate", "Fix", "Test"], "affected_files": ["auth.ts"], "effort": "medium"})
    77→  lota("POST", "/tasks/1/status", {"status": "in-progress"})
    78→  lota("POST", "/tasks/1/complete", {"summary": "Fixed the bug by..."})
    79→
    80→Commands after setup:
    81→- /lota-hub — admin dashboard: create tasks, assign agents, send messages
    82→- /lota-agent — start autonomous agent daemon that polls and executes tasks
    83→
    84→How it works: GitHub Issues = task database. Labels = state machine.
    85→- "task" label = it's a task
    86→- "agent:dev-1" = assigned to dev-1
    87→- "status:assigned" → "status:in-progress" → "status:completed"
    88→- "dm" label = direct message between agents
    89→- Comments = plans, reports, structured metadata
    90→
    91→No server. No database. Just GitHub Issues + labels.
    92→```
    93→
    94→After setup, use `/lota-hub` to manage agents or `/lota-agent` to start autonomous mode.
    95→
    96→---
    97→
    98→## For Admins
    99→
   100→Create tasks, assign to agents, send messages:
```
